# CLAUDE.md — Docvex application

This file provides guidance to Claude Code (claude.ai/code) when working with code in this repository.

> **Scope:** This file documents the **Docvex application** — the Electron
> desktop app and its web-app variant served under `/app`. The separate
> **marketing website** (docvex.ro, source in `landing/`) has its own guide:
> [`landing/CLAUDE.md`](landing/CLAUDE.md). Don't conflate the two. The app is
> DESKTOP ONLY: its web variant (`src/web.jsx`, `docs/app/`) was removed on
> 2026-09-27. The site publishes into the GitHub Pages `docs/` folder.

> **Note (2026-06):** Migration 031 removed the cloud file store and the
> GitHub-style branching/change-request system described in older versions of
> this doc. Files are now local-only per project (`lib/localFolder.js` +
> `.docvex.json` sidecar via `lib/localBranchMeta.js`). The provider stack,
> routing, and Supabase schema below reflect the post-pivot state. Newer
> surfaces — Hub (project list at `/projects`; the standalone `/launch` hub was
> removed), Doc Viewer (`/doc-viewer`, incl. the AI "Generate" advisor), Admin,
> Settings, Mail, the Project AI hub — exist but aren't documented in
> depth here; read the source directly.

Docvex is a team-collaboration DESKTOP app (Electron) for projects (chat, AI
tools, legal newsfeed) on top of Supabase (auth, Postgres + RLS, Realtime,
Edge Functions). Project files live in a local case folder per project, opened
from its `<Project name>.docvex` project file and served from a machine-local
SQLite index — see "Project data" below and `src/projectIndex/README.md` (the
contract). There is no cloud file store.

## Commands

```powershell
npm start                 # electron-forge start (dev + Vite HMR + DevTools open)
                          # NOTE: renderer + preload hot-reload on save, but a change to
                          # src/main.js does NOT restart Electron (Forge's Vite plugin has
                          # main hot-restart stubbed out) — stop and rerun npm start, or a
                          # new IPC handler answers "stale_app" (lib/platform's legisCall)
npm run start:multi       # launch several dev instances in parallel (scripts/run-many.mjs;
                          # sets DOCVEX_ALLOW_MULTI=1 to skip the single-instance lock)
npm run package           # build app folder in out/ (no installer)
npm run make              # build platform installers (Squirrel.exe on Windows)
npm run publish           # make + upload artifacts to GitHub Releases as a draft
                          # — requires GITHUB_TOKEN env var with public_repo scope

npm run test:index        # the project index's tests (node:sqlite → run under Electron-as-Node)

# Marketing site (static HTML in landing/home/, no build — see landing/CLAUDE.md):
npm run site:dev          # serve landing/home with Vite → http://localhost:5175
npm run site:deploy       # scripts/landing-deploy.mjs → copy landing/home into docs/

# Release workflow (uses npm-version lifecycle hooks defined in package.json):
npm run release:patch     # bump x.x.(x+1), commit, tag, push, publish, regenerate web
npm run release:minor     # bump x.(x+1).0
npm run release:major     # bump (x+1).0.0
npm run release:status    # show working-tree status + last commit

# release:* run preversion (fail if dirty), `version` (sync README +
# scripts/release-upload-page.mjs: rebuild the
# phone upload page and copy upload.html + robots.txt into docs/, staged into
# the release commit — only those files, not a full site:deploy), then postversion → scripts/post-release.mjs,
# which runs each step independently (a failure in one doesn't skip the rest):
#   1. git push --follow-tags
#   2. electron-forge publish   — Win Setup.exe + nupkg → draft GitHub release
#   3. publish-mac-zips         — packages + signs both darwin .app bundles, uploads zips
#   4. generate-release-notes   — `claude` CLI summarises commits, PATCHes release body (best-effort)
#   5. finalize-release         — draft=false + rebinds tag_name to v<version> so
#                                  /releases/download/v<x>/* and update.electronjs.org
#                                  start serving it. Needs GITHUB_TOKEN; publish the
#                                  draft manually on github.com as a fallback.

# Repair an existing release's macOS assets (must run ON A MAC):
npm run fix:mac           # rebuild + ad-hoc re-sign + re-zip + replace the
                          # darwin .zip assets on the latest release
npm run fix:mac -- v7.2.5 # ...on a specific tag. Needs GITHUB_TOKEN.
```

> **macOS code-signing — read before cutting a release.** Two mutually
> exclusive paths, chosen by whether `APPLE_SIGNING_IDENTITY` is set:
>
> - **Developer ID + notarization (preferred).** Set `APPLE_SIGNING_IDENTITY`
>   to a `Developer ID Application: … (TEAMID)` cert in the login keychain,
>   plus notarization creds — either `APPLE_API_KEY` / `APPLE_API_KEY_ID` /
>   `APPLE_API_ISSUER` (App Store Connect API key, preferred for CI) **or**
>   `APPLE_ID` / `APPLE_APP_SPECIFIC_PASSWORD` / `APPLE_TEAM_ID`. Then
>   `forge.config.js` has electron-packager sign with the Hardened Runtime +
>   `build/entitlements.mac.plist`, notarize, and staple the ticket, so the
>   build launches with **no Gatekeeper "Apple could not verify …" block**.
>   `make-mac-zips.mjs` then verifies + zips as-is (it must NOT re-sign, which
>   would strip the notarization). Because the FusesPlugin flips fuse bytes in
>   `packageAfterCopy` (before packager's signing step), the signature covers
>   the flipped bytes and stays valid — so `resetAdHocDarwinSignature` is
>   turned OFF on this path. Still Mac-only (codesign/notarytool are macOS).
> - **Ad-hoc (fallback, when the identity is unset).** The build is ad-hoc
>   signed only, so Gatekeeper shows "Apple could not verify …" and users must
>   "Open Anyway" once. electron-forge's FusesPlugin flips fuse bytes AFTER the
>   ad-hoc sign, invalidating the Electron Framework signature — on Apple
>   Silicon the app gets SIGKILLed at launch (`Code Signature Invalid`). The
>   fix is a full `codesign --deep` re-sign in `make-mac-zips.mjs`, which
>   **only works on macOS**, so darwin artifacts MUST be built/signed on a Mac
>   (`npm run fix:mac` to repair an existing release). Two gotchas the scripts
>   already handle: sign in a `/tmp` copy (an iCloud-synced folder keeps
>   re-applying the `com.apple.FinderInfo` xattr that codesign rejects), and
>   stamp the rebuilt bundle with `DOCVEX_APP_VERSION` or the updater re-prompts
>   forever. The in-app self-updater also re-signs each download on the user's
>   Mac as a safety net.
>
> `npm run fix:mac` honours the same env vars — set them and it repairs an
> existing release's darwin zips as notarized builds. Notarization is a
> Developer ID feature ($99/yr Apple Developer Program); without the cert only
> the ad-hoc path is available. (The `.dmg` from `maker-dmg` isn't separately
> notarized, but the `.app` inside it carries its own stapled ticket, so it
> still launches clean.)

No linter (`npm run lint` is a stub). Tests: `npm test` runs the `node:test` suites under `tests/` (plain Node, no DOM — suites: the CAEN letter picker (`tests/caenPicker.test.mjs`), the project index client (`tests/projectIndexClient.test.mjs`) and the pseudonymisation vault (`tests/pseudonymize.test.mjs`) — each bundles its module with esbuild first).

## Tech stack

- **Electron 42** + **Electron Forge 7** (Vite plugin orchestrates main / preload / renderer Vite builds)
- **React 19** + **react-router-dom 7** (MemoryRouter on Electron, BrowserRouter with `basename=/app` on web)
- **Supabase JS 2** — auth, Postgres + RLS, Realtime, Edge Functions
- **pdf.js 5** for in-app PDF preview (`pdfjs-dist`), **html2canvas** for the Report-a-Problem screenshot capture
- **tesseract.js** (+ `@tesseract.js-data/ron`, `/eng`) — local OCR with word positions for the Doc Viewer's image "Extract text" (`lib/textRegions.js`); runtime files copied to `public/ocr/` on postinstall. **libphonenumber-js** + **country-flag-icons** back the identity record's phone field.
- **docx-preview** for rendering `.docx` files (Doc Viewer / `lib/openDocxWindow.js`); lazy-imported so its weight isn't paid until a Word doc is opened
- **Document generation/export** (lazy-imported): **docx** + **pptxgenjs** + **xlsx** (SheetJS) + **jspdf** build real Office files in `lib/documentGen.js` (the AI "Generate" feature — Anthropic Skills sandbox path with a local-builder fallback); **jspdf + html2canvas** power `lib/exportPdf.js` (the office-preview "Convert to PDF"). **word-extractor** (main process) reads legacy `.doc`.
- **react-markdown + remark-gfm** for rendered release notes
- **update-electron-app** → `update.electronjs.org` feed for packaged auto-updates
- **`doc-ai` Edge Function** — Claude (OCR) + OpenAI Whisper (audio transcription) powering the Doc Viewer's "Extract text" and captions tools

## High-level architecture

`forge.config.js` runs three Vite configs (main, preload, renderer). The Vite plugin injects `MAIN_WINDOW_VITE_DEV_SERVER_URL` / `MAIN_WINDOW_VITE_NAME` globals into the main process — `src/main.js` reads them to decide dev-server vs file-loaded bundle.

**Background helper (`src/backgroundWorker.js`).** A third Forge build entry, forked by main.js as an Electron utility process on first use. The project index (`src/projectIndex/`: synchronous SQLite, reconciles, file hashing) and legacy `.doc` parsing (`doc:extract-text`) run there, so they can't freeze the windows. main.js's `projectIndexService()` returns a proxy with the service's methods (`PROJECT_INDEX_METHODS`), each forwarded over the parent port; index events come back as `broadcast` messages and project folders as `projectDir` (→ `registerLocalfileRoot`), in order on the same port. If the helper can't start the index runs in-process as before; quitting waits ≤1.5s for it to flush. A new index method must be added to `PROJECT_INDEX_METHODS`.

`src/renderer.jsx` is the (only) entry. The provider stack is:

```
MemoryRouter
  AuthProvider
    ThemeProvider            — needs auth (per-user theme key); outermost so
                              data-theme is on <html> before first paint
    AppPrefsProvider
      SelectedProjectProvider — per-user storage; auto-clears on access loss
        UpdatesProvider
          NotificationsProvider — source hooks need auth + updates
            ChatUnreadProvider
              <App />
            <NotificationCenter />  — sibling of <App />, toasts at z 9999
```

The former `SplitViewProvider` was removed —
SplitView is now a single-pane shell (`components/SplitView.jsx`) that publishes
its header/footer chrome through `PaneChromeContext` (per-pane, in-memory)
rather than a global multi-pane context.

### Routing

`src/AppRoutes.jsx` defines the full route tree, extracted so it can be rendered both by the main window shell (with the sidebar) and by each SplitView pane (sidebar-less, own MemoryRouter) — `Shell` / `ProjectShell` are passed in as props so the two surfaces can't drift. All page modules are `React.lazy`-imported.

- `/auth` — full-screen, no shell. (The standalone `/launch` hub was removed; the project list at `/projects` is the in-app hub.)
- `/doc-viewer` — full-screen Doc Viewer window (file preview + Legal AI panel), opened from the Files page.
- `/` — wraps everything in `Shell`.
  - Public: `/` (Activity feed), `/versions` (release history; `/updates` redirects here), `/newsletter`, `/notifications` (redirects to `/`), `/invite/:token`.
  - Dev-only: `/debug`.
  - `ProtectedRoute`: `/account`, `/settings`, `/admin`, all `/projects/*`, and project-scoped tools (`/files`, `/clients`, `/todos`, `/chat`, `/generate`, `/automate`, `/ai`, `/mail`) which read the active project from `SelectedProjectContext` rather than a URL param.
  - `/projects/:projectId` wraps `Overview` + `Dashboard` in `ProjectShell`, sharing one fetch and one Realtime channel.

### Main ↔ Renderer IPC contract

`src/preload.js` is the only bridge — exposes `window.electronAPI` via `contextBridge`. Adding any main capability requires editing both files.

- **OAuth / deep-links:** `oauth:open-external` (send), `oauth:callback-url` (receive); `app:get-startup-deep-link` (one-shot pull of a `docvex://` URL captured from `process.argv` at cold start).
- **Updates:** `app:get-version`, `app:is-packaged`, `app:get-platform-info` (`{ platform, arch }`), `update:check`, `update:install`, `update:status` (main → renderer lifecycle events), `update:download-and-install` (macOS self-updater — downloads + extracts + re-signs the arch zip).
- **Window controls (frameless):** `window:minimize / toggle-maximize / close / is-maximized / maximized-changed / is-fullscreen / fullscreen-changed`, and `window:auth-state` (`'locked' | 'app' | 'unlock'` — drives the title-bar chrome before auth). Backs the custom React `TitleBar`. `setZoomFactor` / `getZoomFactor` wrap webFrame zoom (Settings display-scale). **`window:set-fullscreen`** backs the Doc Viewer's **Focus** mode and needs its own path per platform: on macOS `win.setFullScreen` is the whole story (a real fullscreen space, animated, so the handler answers whether the request was ACCEPTED and never reads `isFullScreen()` back), but on Windows every window here is FRAMELESS and a frameless window can accept the call and then sit exactly where it was — which is what made Focus look dead there. So on Windows the result is checked by BOUNDS (Electron may believe it is fullscreen while nothing moved) and, failing, the window is taken to presentation size by hand: `manualFullscreen` in main.js remembers its rect, un-maximizes it, sizes it to `display.bounds` (not `workArea` — that is what puts it over the taskbar) and lifts it `alwaysOnTop`. Two things ride on that: `saveWindowState` REFUSES to record a window while it is presenting (`getNormalBounds()` reports the whole monitor there, and the app would reopen filling the screen), and `hushFullscreen` silences `window:fullscreen-changed` while the swap happens, since the viewer reads that event as the user leaving Focus. The viewer's CROSSING is macOS-only for the same reason: `toggleFocus` fades the document out, waits, then changes the layout and calls the OS — all of which exists because macOS does not composite a window while it animates into its fullscreen space. Off a Mac (`isMac` in lib/platform, `html.is-mac` in renderer.jsx) focus is applied straight away with no fade and no wait: there is nothing to cover, and the settle waits on a fullscreen event the manual path never fires, so the document used to sit out the whole 3s cap.
- **In-app file windows / Doc Viewer:** `app:open-file-window`, `app:open-html-window`, `app:open-docx` (Word fallback chain), `window:open-doc-viewer`; the Doc Viewer multi-tab bus is `doc-viewer:add-file / list / focus / close / tabs`.
- **Upload from a phone (local network):** `phone-upload:start` (`{ dir, project, folder, token?, owner?, port? }` → `{ ok, token, urls, port, computer, expiresAt }`), `phone-upload:stop` (token), `phone-upload:pending` (`{ owner }`), `phone-upload:release` (`{ owner }` — ends every other account's sessions), `phone-upload:event` (main → renderer: start / progress / done / error per file). The server is `src/phoneUploadServer.js` (registered from main.js) — see the phone-upload row in the lib table.
- **Tabs in separate windows:** `window:open-tab-window` (`{ route, title }` → the same app booted at that route, `?tabWindow=1&route=…`; renderer.jsx treats it as NOT the main window — no toasts, no notification sources, no TrayNavigation / LegalFeedSyncRunner), `tab-windows:list` / `tab-windows:changed` (the registry, main.js `tabWindows`), `tab-window:route` (a tab window reports where it is — App.jsx `TabWindowRoute`), `tab-window:focus`, `tab-window:dock` (close the window and send the MAIN window to where it was). In the sidebar every tab — each nav item and each Legislation tab — is wrapped in a MORPH PILL (`TabMenuPill` in Sidebar.jsx, components/useMorphPill, the Files tiles' recipe): hovering shows its name (a Legislation tab: the loaded name) as the custom tooltip, and a RIGHT-CLICK morphs it into a menu — **Open** and **Pop out** (main window only; disabled with a note until the app is restarted onto a preload that has the bridge). A Legislation tab pops out to `<route>?ltab=<tab id>` (the tabs written first with `flushBrowser`; LegalWorkspace selects `ltab` on arrival). The earlier hover buttons were removed, and the open windows are listed in a **Separate windows** section like Open files — a row brings the window forward, its × closes it and brings what it showed back into the main window. Two windows share the Legislation tabs' storage (last write wins). A tab window draws NO app sidebar (AppShell skips the rail's slot and handle, `--sidebar-width: 0`, `.is-tab-window`) and no sidebar burger in the title bar. The title bar in every window reads "DOCVEX | <project> - <tab>" — the sidebar tab the route belongs to (`tabLabelFor` in TitleBar.jsx, the sidebar's labels; the tab alone when no project is selected). NOT verified in the app.
- **Cross-window file sync:** `files:removed` (paths) and `files:changed` broadcast so other windows refresh their listings.
- **Courts' portal + ANAF (main-process, no CORS on either service):** `courts:search` (portal.just.ro `CautareDosare` — case files by number / party / object / court / period, capped at 400 with the total reported), `courts:hearings` (`CautareSedinte` — a court's docket for a day), `anaf:lookup` (`{ cuis }` → ANAF `PlatitorTvaRest` v9, up to 100 CUIs as of today). Court ids come from `lib/courts.json` (built from the WSDL by `scripts/build-courts.mjs`, which writes each court's PLACE properly — "Alba Iulia", "Sectorul 4 București", "Tribunalul pentru minori și familie Brașov" — by looking the id's run-together spelling up in `lib/roLocalities.json` + the counties; `--relabel` redoes the labels without the network).
- **Legislation portal (main-process):** `legislation:search` (SOAP Search), `legislation:archive-put / -list / -get / -remove / -clear` (the offline copy under `userData/legislation`), and **`legislation:page`** (`{ id, fresh }` → `{ ok, html, source, portalError? }`: the act's own page on the portal, `/Public/DetaliiDocument/<id>`, fetched with a browser user agent and kept beside the act as `acts/<id>.html`; answered from that copy without `fresh`, and with `fresh` when the fetch fails).
- **Legacy-format extraction (main-process):** `doc:extract-text` (legacy `.doc` via word-extractor), `whatsapp:prepare-zip / prepare-folder / detect` (WhatsApp export ingest).
- **Dev menus:** `account:switch-to` (dev-only Account menu), `debug:clear-cache`, `debug:send-test-notifications`, `debug:send-email-previews`.
- **External URLs:** `app:open-external` is filtered to `http(s)` only — preserve that filter when adding sibling channels.
- **Protected locations (2026-09-28):** every `local-folder:*` write / move / delete / trash handler and the `localfile://` allow-list go through `protectedPathReason` in main.js — refused: system / program folders, app data (incl. DocVex's own index and vault), the app install, the home folder's hidden entries, drive roots, and (deletes only) the home / Desktop / Documents / Downloads folders themselves; the OS temp folder and macOS iCloud / CloudStorage folders are allowed. Network (UNC) paths are refused unless inside a folder picked in the native dialog or a linked project folder (`trustNetworkRoot`), and are never resolved before that check (NTLM leak). `local-folder:open-path` asks before opening a runnable file (`RUNNABLE_EXT`). `localfile://` grants CORS only to the app's own origins. A new file handler must call `refusePath` too. `app:wipe-local-data` (preload `wipeLocalData`, lib/localWipe) is what Erase data / Delete account use to clear this computer's copy.
- **Local folder (Files page), `local-folder:*`:** `pick / project-dir / list / list-recursive / download / write-files / delete-files / rename-file / create-folder / delete-folder / move / open-path / save-as / extract-archive / show-in-folder / watch / unwatch / changed / read-sidecar / write-sidecar`, plus a recycle-bin set writing a `.docvex-trash/` folder: `trash-file / trash-folder / list-trash / restore-from-trash / delete-from-trash / purge-trash` (sweeps entries > 30 days). Every Trash handler (and the 6-hourly sweep) runs under a per-project lock (`withTrashLock` / `trashLocked` in main.js) and the index `.docvex-trash/.trashmeta.json` is written atomically (temp file + rename): a multi-select delete fires one `trash-file` per file at once, and their read-modify-write of the index used to overwrite each other and leave it invalid JSON — which reads as empty, dropping every item's record. `write-tree` is `write-files`' sibling for a whole FOLDER TREE — each file carries a path relative to the folder and the subfolders are made on the way (account sync's pull); `write-files` sanitises its filenames to a single segment on purpose, so it can't be used for this. The watcher is debounced ~200 ms; only the active folder is watched.

### Custom protocol `docvex://`

Supabase Google OAuth round-trips through Supabase's hosted callback then redirects to `docvex://auth/callback?code=...`. The OS routes it back:

- **Windows:** single-instance lock + `app.on('second-instance', argv => …)` finds the URL in `argv`.
- **macOS:** `app.on('open-url', ...)`.
- **Cold-start race:** if the app wasn't running, `second-instance` never fires — main scans its own `argv` once at startup and exposes it via `app:get-startup-deep-link`, which the renderer pulls once during AuthContext mount.

Critical dev-mode detail: when `process.defaultApp` is true (under `electron-forge start`), `setAsDefaultProtocolClient` MUST be called with the explicit electron exe + app path, otherwise Windows tries to run `electron.exe "docvex://..."` and treats the URL as a path. See the registration block in `src/main.js`.

### Supabase auth flow

`src/lib/supabaseClient.js` sets `flowType: 'pkce'` and `detectSessionInUrl: false`. PKCE → `?code=...` query (parseable via `URLSearchParams`) instead of `#access_token=...` fragment which is awkward in a custom-scheme callback. Disabling `detectSessionInUrl` prevents supabase-js from poking at `window.location`, which is meaningless under MemoryRouter.

`AuthContext` listens for `oauth:callback-url` and calls `supabase.auth.exchangeCodeForSession(code)` itself.

`eraseData()` (distinct from `signOut()`) calls `signOut({ scope: 'global' })` to revoke refresh tokens server-side across all devices, then wipes this computer's copy (`lib/localWipe.js` → main's `app:wipe-local-data`: project index databases, vaults, thumbnails, the index key, Chromium storage; plus every `docvex*` / `sb-*` / `supabase.*` localStorage key). The index's private / knowledge / settings columns and the thumbnail cache are sealed at rest (`src/projectIndex/seal.js`, AES-256-GCM, key in `userData/index-key.bin` wrapped by safeStorage; main loads it and hands it to the background helper in its `init` message, since safeStorage exists only in the main process). Erase data awaits the helper's close, ends it and forgets the key. `deleteAccount()` calls the `delete-user` Edge Function with the user's JWT.

### Custom `localfile://` protocol

Registered with privileges `standard | secure | supportFetchAPI | stream | bypassCSP | corsEnabled` so `<img src>`, `<video src>`, AND `fetch()` from the Vite dev origin all work. The renderer URL-encodes the full path as one segment; `protocol.handle('localfile', …)` decodes it, streams via `fs.createReadStream` wrapped in a `Response` (so `<video>` byte-range works without buffering whole videos in memory). A `?thumb=N` query returns an OS-downscaled thumbnail (cached, bounded) for image/doc tiles; HTTP Range requests are honoured (206) for media seeking.

### Auto-update pipeline

Two layers running in parallel — don't confuse them:

**Releases live in a PUBLIC repo of their own** — `release-repo.json` at the root (today `docvex/docvex_releases`) is read by main.js, UpdatesContext, forge.config.js (publisher + Squirrel icon URL now `https://docvex.ro/favicon.ico`), the release scripts (`scripts/releaseRepo.mjs`), the website's installers page and README, so the SOURCE repo can be private. That repo needs at least one commit (a release tag is created at its default branch). Installs from before this change still poll the old repo: keep `docvex/docvex` public (or mirror one release there) until they have updated.

1. **`update-electron-app` in `src/main.js`** (packaged builds only) — polls `update.electronjs.org` every 10 min, which reads GitHub Releases for the releases repo. Squirrel.Windows downloads in background, installs on next launch.
2. **`UpdatesContext` in renderer** — fetches the releases repo's `https://api.github.com/repos/<owner>/<name>/releases` (cached in `sessionStorage` under `docvex:releases-cache:v1`, 1 h TTL) and subscribes to `update:status` events from main. Drives the sidebar badge + the Updates page banner.

Layer 1 is the source of truth for "is an installer downloaded and ready?" → `update-downloaded` → `update:status { state: 'downloaded' }` → renderer shows "Restart & install". Layer 2 shows release notes + version-mismatch. Works in dev too (no Squirrel needed). Web returns `state: 'web'`.

**Windows-only Squirrel.** Layer 1 is gated on `AUTO_UPDATE_SUPPORTED` (`process.platform === 'win32'`) — the macOS build isn't Developer-ID signed, so Squirrel.Mac can't apply updates. `update:check` returns `{ state: 'unsupported' }` on macOS/Linux.

**macOS self-updater.** `update:download-and-install` (main) downloads the arch-correct release zip (`installerAssetFor` in `UpdatesContext`), extracts with `ditto`, strips xattrs + ad-hoc re-signs (`xattr -cr` then `codesign --force --deep --sign -`) to repair the fuse-invalidated signature, then a detached script swaps the `.app` and relaunches (with rollback). Progress flows back as `update:status { state: 'downloading', percent }` → `'installing'`. On failure it falls back to a browser download (`downloadUpdate`); Linux always uses that fallback.

Semver compare is a tiny inline `semverGT()` in `UpdatesContext.jsx` — `major.minor.patch` only, strips `v` prefix and pre-release suffix.

## Design system

`src/styles/designSystem.css` + `src/lib/designSystem.js` + the **Design system** tab (`pages/DesignSystem`, `/design`, System section of the sidebar, signed-in). ONE set of tokens the chrome is built from — bars (`--ds-bar-h/-radius/-border/-frost/-blur`), controls (`--ds-control-h/-radius/-size/-tint/-tint-hover/-blur`, `--ds-search-w/-w-focus`), type (eyebrow / title / kicker / label sizes and tracking), pills and tags, surfaces (card / row / menu radius, `--ds-hairline`, `--ds-content-max`) — declared on `:root` with the app's CURRENT values, and read by the shared recipes with the same value as a fallback (`height: var(--ds-control-h, 24px)`): the Legislation tab bar and its search / toggle / tool button, the Legislation bar (`components/LegalBar`: field row, switch, picker, menu, date fields, note) and tags, the PageMasthead (eyebrow, title, kicker, compact bar), the Files search, the CAEN bottom bar. Nothing moves until a token is changed. **ONE FAMILY for the whole app** (there were three — Personal, Project following the Files tab, Viewer following the Doc Viewer — folded into one at the user's request so everything renders the same): the Legislation tab's rules (no card fills, rows on the page ground with a hairline, generous gaps). `DS_FAMILIES` has the one entry (`personal`, labelled DocVex), `familyOf` always answers it, AppShell and the Doc Viewer still stamp `data-ds` so a second family could return, and the family tokens (`--ds-card-fill/-shadow`, `--ds-section-gap`, `--ds-row-density`) are declared once under `[data-ds="personal"]`. **Pills are ONE recipe** (the tab's **Pills** section shows every one): `--ds-pill-h` / `-pad` / `-radius` / `-weight` / `-tint` / `-ring` + `--ds-tag-size` (10.4px) / `-tracking`, sentence case; a tag is SOFT (`--pill-tone` tint + ring, the text in the tone: `.lg-tag` info, `.cn-tag` info / `.is-old` warning, `.dsg-status` success, `.lgt-status-pill` success / warning / danger — a button too, its tint deepening on hover); every pill is soft, none filled. **Callouts** (`.cn-callout`) have no fill and no coloured rule — a hairline box on the page ground, a tone dot before the title (accent = note, warning = `.is-old`). Colour is NOT a token here — it stays with tokens.css. **Overrides** a device keeps (`docvex:design-system:v1`) are applied to <html> at boot (`initDesignSystem` in renderer.jsx) and on every change, family tokens as a `<style id="ds-family-overrides">` rule; `overridesToCss` exports them for a release. **The tab** shows the families and their rules, an AI composer (`askDesign` → project-ai `ask`, sonnet, `usageAction: 'design-system'`: the catalogue with current values + the family rules in, JSON `{ changes, note }` out — proposed, applied only on Apply), the GALLERY (the real recipes stood still — the tab bar, the Legislation tabs' workspace (`components/LegalWorkspace`), bottom bar, field row, dropdown, pills, buttons, row, section, callouts, empty state, masthead — stamped with the family being looked at) and the token table (type a value → app-wide at once; reset per token / all; export as CSS). **PAGE LAYOUT** (the tab's **Page layout** section, `PageLayoutSample` / `.dsg-pl*`; the **Page layout** token group, read off the Playbook and the Legislation tab): `--ds-page-inset` (= `--chrome-inset`, the sidebar's window-edge gap — where sticky things stick, where a side list ends), `--ds-rail-gap` (= `--rail-gap`), `--ds-content-gap` (= `--content-left-gap`), `--ds-divider-pull` (content gap − rail gap: how far the header's divider and a side list's left edge run out toward the sidebar), `--ds-head-gap` 8px (under the divider), `--ds-list-w` 236px, `--ds-list-gap` 20px (list ↔ content, and the content's right padding), `--ds-page-foot` 64px (the air at the end, inside the last section). Playbook.css (`--pbk-*` aliases on `.pbk-rules-split`) and LegalWorkspace.css read them with today's values as fallbacks. The gallery also stands the **Toggle** (`components/Toggle`, live) and the **Danger zone** (`components/DangerZone` + `DangerRow` + `.dz-btn`). The gallery also stands **Legal highlights** (`LegalHighlightsSample`, `.dsg-refs*`): every mark drawn on text read as Romanian law — the Word preview's act / split-run / CAEN / CUI / cross-reference (rest, hover, pressed) / landing marks (MIRRORED in DesignSystem.css under `.dsg-refdoc`, since DocViewer.css is bound to its window — keep in step), the picture text-layer marks, and the Legislation tab's live `.lg-ref` kinds + `.lg-hit` find. The gallery also stands the Advisor's **File pills** (`.aichat-attach-chip`, ProjectAIChat.css imported: attached in the composer with ×, and `.is-static` on a sent message — a folder path cut with …) and the **AI edit card** (`.ai-edit`, components/AiEdits: edited · Undo, undone, not changed). And the **Reply choices** (components/AiChoices, live: an AI reply's options as pills under its text; disabled while answering). The page's **Tooltips** section (`TooltipsSamples`, the real `.tooltip` / morph-pill classes held still by `.dsg-tips`) shows every way the custom tooltip renders — one line, two lines or more, the right-click menu, the danger confirm, the prompt, the Doc Viewer's highlight pill per kind of mark and the CARD a highlight click expands into (their styles are `components/RefPill.css`, shared by the viewer and the gallery). EVERY pill shape — hover, menu, submenu, confirm, prompt — and the NOTIFICATION CARDS stand on the one-line tooltip's ground (`--bg-sidebar`; `--bg-card` read blue on Ink), row hovers a neutral text-colour wash; the danger confirm is the DANGER ZONE's card to the recipe (`.dz-card` / `.dz-hd` / `.dz-row` / `.dz-btn`: 38% border, the 3 → 13% sweep on 9s, the band and icon tile, a flat fill on the button's hover, no pointer light) over that ground. The **Notifications** section (`NotificationsSample`) draws the real `NotificationToast` for every sample in `notifications/testNotifications` (+ a file toast with its glyph), persistent and held in a grid. The gallery's **Tooltip pill** states the rule every tooltip follows: one line → fully rounded ends; TWO LINES OR MORE → HALF the corner radius (a quarter of the one-line pill's height), MEASURED — Tooltip.jsx inline, useMorphPill's `tallHover` → `.tooltip.is-multiline` — so markup content (the Doc Viewer's highlight pill) and wrapped lines count. The page's **Empty fields** section (`EmptyFieldsSample`, `.dsg-fieldsdoc` — DocViewer.css's `.dv-field` rules MIRRORED, keep in step; `.dcx-inline` live) shows every blank state: the empty slot, under a hovered paragraph, Word's underlined gap, a blank shown as its text, filled, a party preview, in the picked paragraph (filled / being worked on), the inputs (empty with the placeholder, filled, per-person colours) and the shapes read as blanks. The Files toolbar's **Highlights sample** (`lib/highlightsSample.js`, `fxAddHighlightsSample` in ProjectFiles) writes a Word document holding every highlight and blank shape (checked against lib/lawRefs line by line) into the folder on show. Under it, **Platform colours** (`PlatformColoursSample`, `.dsg-plat-*`): each platform's own colour (All = accent, then `PLATFORMS[…].tone` in `PLATFORM_ORDER`) with its dot, site and token, and its filters (`FACETS`) drawn as the live `.lgb-pill`s — read from the data, so changing a tone there changes the sample. The gallery also stands the **Side drawer** (`components/SideDrawer` + `.css`, `sd-`; `SideDrawerSample`, live): a panel sliding in from the right over the page (fixed, portalled; the sidebar's surface; 280ms; kept mounted after its first opening), resizable by its left edge (`widthKey` keeps it per device, `minWidth` / `maxWidth`, double-click = `defaultWidth`), a head with the title / `subtitle`, Back (`onBack`), `headerExtra` and ×; Escape or a press outside closes it except inside `keepOpenWithin`; `deferContent` draws the children only once the slide has landed; `contentKey` scrolls the body to its top. Research's drawer is built on it — **use it for any side preview** (the Playbook's WordDrawer is the older copy it came from). The gallery also stands the **drop zone** (`components/DropZone`, the Timeline / Playbook / record sources import surface) full and compact, live (dropped files are only named). The gallery also holds DESIGNS not yet on any tab: the **calendar** (`components/BarCalendar` + `.css`, `bc-`) — `BarDatePicker` (one date) and `BarDateRange` (from – to in one field), built on the Legislation bar's field (`.lg-input`) and hanging their calendar from it on the Kind list's ground (`.lg-menu`, portalled, placed from the field's rect, closed by a press elsewhere / Escape / a scroll); the date written zz.ll.aaaa, weeks from Monday, today ringed, the chosen day filled in the accent, days either side faded but pickable; the range is ALWAYS TWO CALENDARS side by side — FROM left, TO right — a press in either setting that end (TO can't go before FROM nor FROM after TO: `Calendar`'s `min` / `max` bound the days AND the navigation — the month arrows stop at the bound's month, `canPrev` / `canNext`, greyed; the keys stop at the bound day; a month opened outside is pulled back in); with only one end chosen the other calendar shows it FADED (`.bc-day.is-ghost`); once BOTH are chosen the span is ONE CONTINUOUS BAND joining them on both pages (`band` on — across two months it runs to FROM's page edge and on from TO's), fading in as it appears (`band` switching on, like `reveal`, → `.bc.is-reveal` ~420ms), and hovering a day previews moving that end, band and all; the labels over the calendars are just "From" / "To"; changing MONTH slides the title and day grid in from the side the month moved to — the Activity feed entry to the letter (300ms, 28px, a fade; keyed by month; `.bc-grid/.bc-title.is-enter-right|left`, the calendar clipping it at its sides); Clear empties both; a press sets THAT END only — no swapping, no closing on a pick; the popover is re-fitted to the window at every step (`useCalendarPopover(inline, relayout)`); the popover grows to the pair's width (`.bc-pop.is-range`), the calendars stand straight on the folder's ground — no box, border or fill of their own (`.bc-range-cals`) — with Clear in the BOTTOM-RIGHT corner (the foot's right end, after the quick picks), the spans share the full width under them, and every calendar popover is fitted to the window once drawn (pulled left, grown upward when there is no room below, else raised); TWO SEPARATE SECTIONS: the FIELD (the button — ending in the DROPDOWN's chevron, LegalBar's `.lg-kind-chev` as `.bc-field-chev`, turning over when open; open, its edge turns accent and it keeps its hover wash, `.bc-field.is-open:hover`) and, open, the CALENDAR, a box of its own 6px under it (`GAP`), never joined — one accent edge all round, four equal rounded corners, `width: max-content` so it always fits its content (and NO `max-width: 100%` on a floating one — its 100% is the field's width, which cut the background short of the calendars), no shadow; switching is a plain FADE (`bc-fade-in` once placed, `.is-placed`; `bc-fade-out`, `.is-closing`, `FOLD_MS` = 150 in the JSX, removed after it) — a scroll / resize and reduced motion close at once; drawn in place (the gallery) the calendar floats under its field over the content (`.bc-pop.is-inline`, absolute) so opening moves nothing. (A joined 'folder' shape — field as tab, calendar as body — was tried and replaced by this at the user's request.) The spans sit under a **Quick picks** label (`.bc-quick-label`). — with Last 7 / 30 days, This year, Last year and Clear under both; keys ←/→ a day, ↑/↓ a week, PageUp/PageDown a month, Enter picks, Escape closes (focus follows the day only once the keys are used, so a calendar drawn open never steals focus); ISO dates in and out, `inline` draws it open in place — shown open in the gallery and in "Legislation bar, with dates", the Legislation bar as it would stand with a period and a day. **The DOC VIEWER's chrome is in the language too** (the tab's **DocVex file viewer** section, `DocViewerSample` / `.dsg-dv*`; the **Doc Viewer** token group, `--ds-dv-*`): the viewer window's floating cards (the side panel, Quick actions, the page list — inset, corner radius, frost, blur), the panel's default width, the document's clearance from the panel (`--ds-dv-doc-gap`, added to the panel's width = `--dv-doc-inset`), the page list's width, and the dark-glass pills over the document (button size, glass, blur, the find bar's top and open width, the counters' type) — READ OFF THE WORD DOCUMENT'S LAYOUT, which is the reference, and read by pages/DocViewer.css with the Word values as fallbacks. The point: every kind of file now renders by the same chrome — the find bar top-RIGHT for every kind (text, slides and sheets had it top-left), ONE footprint for the document (`--dv-doc-inset` everywhere; text, slides, pictures, records and the video controls kept 8px more, and a sheet had none and sat under the panel), the media zoom pill where the document pill stands, the slides' page counter the Word counter's pill, the Quick actions card for every kind (see the quick-actions row). The sample is built from the tokens (the viewer's stylesheet is bound to its window); the values read off the layout are listed under it. **THE SCROLLBAR is ONE recipe** (the tab's **Scrollbar** sample + the **Scrollbar** token group: `--ds-scrollbar-size / -radius / -min / -thumb / -thumb-hover / -thumb-active / -track`): index.css's `::-webkit-scrollbar` rules read them, and so do the rail's faded thumb (Sidebar.css) and the overlay thumbs (the chat thread's `.aichat-*scrollbar-thumb`, the Doc Viewer's `.dv-tabbar-thumb`). NOTHING may set `scrollbar-width: thin` or `scrollbar-color` (Chromium switches the webkit recipe off for an element that does — every such rule was removed); `scrollbar-width: none` to hide a bar is fine. **Adding an element to the language = make its recipe read the tokens (fallback = its current value) and stand it in the gallery.** Recipes NOT yet reading the tokens keep their literal values: the Files path bar and bottom bar's own rules, Chat's toolbar and composer, the Doc Viewer's panels — wire them the same way.

## Theme system

`src/styles/tokens.css` defines all colour tokens. Two themes: **Cream** (default) and **Ink** (dark variant). Selectors are scoped via `[data-theme="…"]` on `<html>`, set by `ThemeContext`. Brand constants live on bare `:root`, semantic aliases live on `:root[data-theme="cream|ink"]`.

The unsuffixed `:root` block also paints in Cream so the first frame before React mounts isn't a flash. The bare `[data-theme="…"]` selector (without `:root`) lets ANY element declare its own subtree theme — `ThemePicker.jsx` uses this so each preview card paints in its own theme regardless of the app's active theme.

**Brand palette:** `--color-ink`, `--color-slate`, `--color-sand`, `--color-cream`, `--color-cognac`.

**Semantic tokens** (every component reads via `var(...)` — no hex literals):

```
--bg-page / --bg-card / --bg-elevated / --bg-sidebar / --bg-input
--border / --border-strong
--text-primary / --text-secondary / --text-muted / --text-on-accent
--accent / --accent-hover / --accent-soft / --accent-tint
--danger / --danger-soft / --danger-text
--success / --success-soft
--warning / --warning-soft
--info / --info-soft
--shadow-card / --shadow-elev
--overlay-scrim / --scrollbar-thumb
```

**Notification category palette:** `--cat-auth / project / member / file / role / update / support / system` (darker on Cream, lighter on Ink so each category reads at a glance in the toast stack + history view).

**Typography:** `--font-body` = Inter; `--font-display` = Plus Jakarta Sans. Loaded via `<link>` in `index.html` / `index.web.html`.

**Icons use the Ink palette in both themes:** a block at the foot of tokens.css re-declares Ink's accent / category / status tokens on every `svg` (and `*-icon` / `*glyph` / `.icon` wrappers, not buttons or links) under Cream, so icons keep their dark-theme colours; `--text-*` is left alone so text-coloured icons still read on light surfaces. Cream also splits `--bg-backdrop` (the window backdrop, seams and fades — near-white #F9F9FA) from `--bg-page` (cream, for elements), and paints the top bar + sidebar `--bg-bars` (neutral near-white #F4F4F5).

Adding a third theme = 30-line addition (new `:root[data-theme="…"]` block + entry in `ThemeContext`'s themes list).

## Graphics quality + load time (Settings → Optimization)

**Graphics presets** (`lib/perf.js`, `lib/usePerf.js`, `styles/perf.css`): Auto (default) · Ultra · High · Medium · Low, kept PER DEVICE (`docvex.perf.v1`, not the per-account prefs). A preset resolves to a level stamped as `data-perf` on <html> by `initPerf()` in renderer.jsx BEFORE the first render. Auto reads the GPU name (WebGL), cores and memory (`detectPerf`), cached in `docvex.perf.detected.v1` so boot never creates a WebGL context (re-checked on idle 20 s in). What each level drops: HIGH — looping decorative animations (the lawref shimmer, AI-tag / danger shimmers, pulses) and blur on still grounds; MEDIUM — ALL `backdrop-filter` (surfaces that relied on it raised to ~96% opaque, frost tokens overridden), mouse-FOLLOWING by the cursor spotlight and the sidebar / rail glows (they stay drawn, still — `CursorSpotlight follow={false}` stays centred; the dot grid is never removed), a lighter `--shadow-elev`; LOW — also the shadow tokens and Minimize motion (perf.js ORs Low into `data-reduce-motion`; the user's own choice is kept in `data-user-reduce-motion`). The reduce-motion rule in index.css now spares spinners and indeterminate progress bars (`[class*="spin"]`, `.is-spinning svg`, `.is-busy svg`, `.is-indeterminate`, `.update-progress-bar`). JS effects gate on `perfAllows('spotlight')` at event time (AppShell's CursorSpotlight via `usePerfAllows`, Sidebar `onSpotMove`, the Legislation / LegalBrowser / Playbook rail spotlights, `useItemSpots` — now rAF-batched, reads before writes — FilesWorkspace tab buttons, the Doc Viewer's advisor card / composer / `trackItemSpot`). **A new blur, glow, looping animation or pointer-following effect must be added to perf.css or gated with `perfAllows`.** The FPS counter (`fpsCounter` pref) now defaults to OFF — while shown it redraws every frame.

**Load-time rules** (the Load times card lists them): fonts are bundled (`@fontsource/inter`, `plus-jakarta-sans`, `cinzel` — no Google Fonts `<link>`; don't add remote stylesheets back); `AuthWindowGate` reveals the main window at once when a Supabase session with a refresh token is stored (the network refresh no longer holds the window hidden; a revoked session still hands over to the sign-in window); `preloadBootRoute()` fetches the Files chunk before render; AppShell waits 6 s before `preloadRoutes()` / `warmLegalData()` and both run on true idle only (no `timeout`), the CAEN notes are no longer warmed; the Files listing has no debounce while nothing is on screen; `legalBrowser.js` loads `legalOmni` on first `applyPage` (keeps lawRefs + legislation out of the startup bundle — don't re-add a static import from anything the Sidebar imports); main.js: DevTools only from the local dev server (`DEVTOOLS_ALLOWED` = `!!MAIN_WINDOW_VITE_DEV_SERVER_URL`: F12 / Ctrl+Shift+I, right-click Inspect, the macOS View menu and the launch auto-open are all gated, and a `devtools-opened` backstop closes them anywhere else — a packaged build has no DevTools at all), the Squirrel check starts 30 s after ready, the Explorer verb is registered 15 s in and only when `userData/openwith.json` differs, the Doc Viewer pre-warm waits 12 s; UpdatesContext fetches GitHub releases 5 s in (a cache hit still hydrates at once); Vite: renderer `target: 'chrome130'`, main `minify: true`. Measured: renderer entry chunk 832 KB → 760 KB.

**ONE POINTER for every mouse-following effect** (`lib/pointer.js`): a single window listener; once a frame every subscriber's `read(p)` runs (rects), then every `write(p, read)` (styles) — a write returning true asks for another frame (eased lights). NO effect may keep its own mousemove / pointermove listener: `subscribePointer` for custom work, `registerHoverSpot(selector)` for a control's `--item-spot-x/y` hover light, `lib/pointerSpots.js` for surface lights (`useRailSpotlight`, `registerElementSpot` / `useElementSpot`, `useMiniGlowSpot`). Surface lights are INJECTED elements (`.spot-light.spot-glow` / `.spot-shine` › `.spot-dot`, base rules in `styles/spotLights.css`, each surface's own look in its stylesheet) moved by transform — never a gradient centred on a custom property; `.is-live` (`will-change`) only while moving, and never over unfrosted sharp text (`.mini-glow:not(.is-pinned)`, composers, menus — a GPU layer there drops the text's LCD smoothing). The Sidebar, CursorSpotlight, Tooltip and useMorphPill run on it too (tooltips: React state only shows / hides; the pointer moves them). Functional handlers (drags, pans, resizes, marquee, scrubbing) keep their own listeners. `--item-spot-x/y` and `--spot-x/y` are registered non-inheriting in index.css. HOVER CSS: never write a hover rule ending in `> :not(…)` / `*` (no specific target) — ONE such rule anywhere makes Chromium restyle the whole subtree of every element whose :hover changes, app-wide (six in FilesWorkspace.css were exactly this). HOVER FADES OF PAINT PROPERTIES: a `transition` of `background` / `box-shadow` / `border-color` that a `:hover` sets off repaints AND re-layers the whole page on every frame of the fade (Chromium's paint + layerize walk the page, not the item), and a fast sweep keeps several running — measured in Electron over the Files grid (150 tiles): ~1.5 s of render work per sweep. On REPEATED items in big pages the hover look is PRE-DRAWN on `::after` ABOVE the content (border + shadow only, `pointer-events: none`) and faded by `opacity` — composited, ~0.45 s — while a fill switches without a fade (`.fx-tile::after`, `.fx-list-row::after`, `.lh-row::after`; states with a look of their own set the layer's `opacity: 0`). Never put that layer UNDER text (a composited layer there takes the text off LCD smoothing for the fade) and never give it a fill (on top, it tints thumbnails — up to 12/255 on Cream). A `color` fade on an element holding a `currentColor` SVG icon is the other expensive hover: every frame restyles the SVG (sidebar tabs, measured: 1.35–4.5 s of style work per sweep vs 0.15 s without the colour fade). So a hover that holds an icon CHANGES ITS COLOUR WITHOUT A FADE — the `color` entry was taken out of 87 such transitions (sidebar tabs, Legislation rail / tabs / tool buttons, title bar, Files toolbar, Doc Viewer and Chat buttons…; background / border fades kept). Text-only hovers keep their colour fade (cheap). Don't add `color` back to the transition of anything with an icon inside.

**Runtime rules (every preset — they change no pixel):** a sticky mini header's PINNED state lives in a small component of its own, measured at most once a frame (`FilesPathbar` in FilesWorkspace, `ActivityToolbar`, `ChatToolbar`, `PjdCompactBar`, `MailCompactBar`, `AdminCompactBar`, `VersionsCompactBar`; LegalTabs rAF-throttles its own) — state on a big page re-renders the whole page, grid included, as the bar fades. Pointer / scroll handlers read every rect first, then write (no read-after-write per item). A light that follows the pointer is a PRE-DRAWN gradient layer MOVED BY TRANSFORM (the Sidebar's `.sidebar-glow` / `.sidebar-shine` dots, like CursorSpotlight) — never a gradient whose centre is a custom property on a container: that repaints the full layer every frame and, inherited, restyles every element inside (that was the sidebar hover lag). Hover tooltips (useMorphPill) follow the cursor by a direct transform write once a frame; React state only shows / hides them. The per-control hover light `--item-spot-x/y` is registered NON-inheriting in index.css (`@property`, initial 50%; `*::before, *::after` take it with `inherit`) — it must only ever be read by the control it is written on or that control's own pseudo-elements. Context values are memoised (Auth, Updates now too). Big lists are memoised rows with stable handlers: Files `Tile` / `Row` (`React.memo`, handlers through latest-ref wrappers; ProjectFiles `reuseList` hands back unchanged item objects), `ActivityRow`, PDF `PdfPage` / `PdfThumb`, the Legislation `RailTab` / `StripTab`; the Sidebar is `React.memo` (AppShell passes stable callbacks) with its account row split out (`SidebarAccount` — the morph pill sets state per mouse move); the Legislation frame reads a narrowed browser snapshot (`useFrameBrowser`) so typing in the address row doesn't re-render it, and `setDraft` / `setTabLoading` don't save; `TextRegionsLayer` builds its lines once per reading and only moves its frame while panning; AI chat Markdown is memoised on the text. Keep new code to the same rules.

**The app origin (V8 code cache)**: packaged builds load every app window from `docvex-app://bundle/index.html` (`loadRendererBundle` in main.js — the ONE loader for app windows; dev still loads the Vite server), a privileged scheme with `codeCache: true`, served by `registerAppOrigin` from the renderer bundle folder (own MIME table — `guessMimeFromName` says text/plain for .js; the CSP, `APP_CSP`, is set on its documents too). Chromium keeps V8's compiled code for it between launches, which file:// never got (measured on the packaged build: the auth window's DOMContentLoaded 208 → 142 ms warm). The ORIGIN changed, so the first launch copies localStorage from file:// (`migrateOriginStorage`, before any window: hidden blank-page windows read file:// and write the app origin, only keys it lacks; `userData/app-origin-migrated.json` marks it done; `migratingOrigin` stops those windows' closing from quitting the app). IndexedDB isn't copied (desktop keeps only rebuildable caches there). It COPIES, so file:// still holds the pre-migration data: `DOCVEX_FILE_ORIGIN=1` loads from file:// as before, and a failed app-origin load falls back to it per window. `DOCVEX_ORIGIN_DEBUG=1` logs every request. Verified on a packaged build against a throwaway `--user-data-dir`: migration, sign-in/prefs keys carried, fonts, no failed requests, code-cache entries written, no re-migration on later launches. NOT measured signed in (the test profile had no session).

**Optimistic UI** — user actions show their result at once and reconcile with the disk / server after; a failure rolls back exactly that change and says so (notify, category + variant 'error', a dedupeKey), and the item looks pending meanwhile (`is-pending`: faded). FILES (`lib/optimisticFiles.js` + ProjectFiles): every mutation — delete, restore, rename, move (drag / cut-paste), new folder, copy-paste, import, new file, delete forever, empty Trash, and undo/redo of each — is a pending OP laid over the last listing (`applyOpsToListing` / `applyOpsToAll` / `applyOpsToTrash`), never an edit of the listing itself; success re-lists first and only then drops the op (`settleOps`), so the old state never flashes back; listing replies are numbered (`listAllFresh` / `listDirFresh`) and a stale one is dropped, so the watcher can't resurrect or duplicate; disk calls go through `callDisk`; multi-select has `onDeleteMany` / `onRestoreMany`. The web build applies only deletions instantly. SUPABASE: chat send / thread reply / DM send (a `tmp-` stand-in row, `mergeIncoming` puts the Realtime echo in its place, `settleTemp` swaps or removes it), chat edit and delete, project rename (list + header) / jurisdiction / AI context, change member role (`onFailed`), edit custom role (`onOptimistic` / `onFailed`), Admin services, Playbook sample removal. DELIBERATELY left waiting on the server: delete project / account, erase data, wipe AI memory, remove member, delete custom role, send / resend / revoke invite, accept invite, sign-in / sign-up / password, the admin allowlist, create project / custom role (they need the server's id), sending mail, Mail disconnect (a false "disconnected" is a security claim), and every AI call, portal search, OCR and read. A new write should follow the same split.

## Context layer

All under `src/context/`. Every hook returns plain objects; no Redux / Zustand. Persistence keys all share the `docvex.*` prefix. `SplitViewContext` was removed (see the provider-stack note above); `PaneChromeContext` is in-memory and per-pane (publishes a pane's header/footer chrome into the SplitView shell via `usePaneChromeSlot`).

| Context | Exported shape (via `useXxx()`) | Persistence |
| --- | --- | --- |
| **AuthContext** | `{ session, loading, lastAuthEvent, signInWithEmail, signUpWithEmail, signInWithGoogle, linkGoogle, setPassword, signOut, logout, eraseData, deleteAccount }` | Supabase-js native (`sb-*` keys); `lastAuthEvent.at` timestamps repeated events so downstream effects can distinguish back-to-back TOKEN_REFRESHED firings. |
| **AppPrefsContext** | `{ prefs: { textSize, thumbnails, reduceMotion, fileView, language, showTokenUsage, fpsCounter }, setPref(key, value), resetPrefs }` — `fpsCounter` ('off' / 'simple' / 'complex' / 'graph', default simple) picks the title bar's `components/FpsMeter` look (Settings → Behavior → FPS counter; no rAF loop runs when off) | `docvex.appPrefs.<userId>` (JSON). Side-effects on change: `data-reduce-motion` on `<html>`, display-scale via `appScale`/`platform.setAppZoom`. |
| **ChatUnreadContext** | `{ unreadCount, markRead() }` | `docvex.chat.lastRead.<userId>.<projectId>` (ISO ts); Realtime sub on `chat_messages` per project. |
| **PaneChromeContext** | `usePaneChromeSlot({description})` (publish), `usePaneChromePortalEl()` / `usePaneChromeFooterEl()` (chrome targets) | None — in-memory, one provider per SplitView pane. |
| **ThemeContext** | `{ theme (resolved `cream`/`ink`), themePreference (`cream`/`ink`/`system`), setTheme, themes: [{ id, label, description, swatchOrder }] }` | `docvex.theme.<userId>` (+ `docvex.theme.last` signed-out fallback). `system` tracks the OS via `matchMedia`. |
| **SelectedProjectContext** | `{ selectedProjectId, selectedProject, loading, selectProject(id, prefetched?), clearSelection, patchSelectedProject(patch), pickerOpen, openPicker, closePicker, togglePicker, switching, switchingToName, beginSwitch(name) }` | `docvex.selectedProject.<userId>` |
| **ProjectContext** (URL-scoped) | `{ project, role, members, customRoles, loading, error, refresh, refreshCustomRoles, removeMemberLocal, setMemberRoleLocal, removeCustomRoleLocal }` | None — Realtime subs + optimistic local mutations |
| **NotificationsContext** | `{ notifications, activeToasts, unreadCount, notify(payload), dismissToast(id), markRead(id), markAllRead, remove(id), clearAll }` | `docvex.notifications.v1.<userId|_anonymous>` (debounced). `HISTORY_CAP = 100`, `MAX_ACTIVE_TOASTS = 3`. |
| **UpdatesContext** | `{ currentVersion, latestVersion, isPackaged, releases, loading, error, hasUpdate, installerState, checkNow, installUpdate }` | `sessionStorage` `docvex:releases-cache:v1` |
| **ReportProblemContext** | `{ open, capturing, screenshot: { blob, dataUrl } | null, captureAndOpen, close, removeScreenshot }` | None; html2canvas is lazy-imported so the first render doesn't pay the cost. |

**Provider-order constraints** to remember:
- ThemeProvider above everything else that renders so `data-theme` is set before first paint.
- AppPrefsProvider sits below ThemeProvider, above SelectedProjectProvider.
- ChatUnreadProvider wraps `<App />` inside NotificationsProvider (chat unread badges). `NotificationCenter` is a sibling of `<App />`, not nested.
- `NotificationCenter` mounts as a sibling of `<App />` so its toasts (z 9999) render above any modal.

## Library layer (`src/lib/`)

| File | Purpose |
| --- | --- |
| `supabaseClient.js` | Singleton supabase-js client (PKCE, no auto-detect-in-URL). |
| `projects.js` | Project CRUD + member listings + auth-user profile upsert. |
| `thumbnails.js` | Byte-level thumbnail generators (canvas / pdf.js / PPTX preview / DOCX render). Only the *fallback* path — see `thumbnailEngine.js`. |
| `thumbnailEngine.js` | The thumbnail system. Turns a file into an ordered list of candidate URLs (OS thumbnail via `localfile://…?thumb=N` → original bytes → renderer-generated blob) that `FileThumbnail` walks on error. Owns the bounded generation queue, single-flight, refcounted blob cache, and failure memo. |
| `pdfCache.js` | Module-level cache of parsed pdf.js documents, keyed by content hash. Evicted from the "Debug → Clear all cached data" menu. |
| `pdfWorker.js` | pdf.js worker entry point used by pdfCache. |
| `localBranchMeta.js` | RETIRED sidecar layer: now `fileIdForPath` / `pathForFileId` over the index's portable ids, plus no-op stand-ins. Legacy `.docvex.json` sidecars are imported into `.docvex/ids.json` (ids kept) and deleted by the main process. |
| `localFolder.js` | Desktop folder API (`localFolderApi.pick / list / download / writeFiles / deleteFiles / renameFile / openPath / showInFolder / …`) plus `projectIndexApi` (the project-index calls, feature-detected), `linkProjectFolder` / `resolveProjectFolder` / `legacyProjectDir`. The web backend (File System Access API, IndexedDB handles) was removed with the web build; `persistPickedHandle` & co. are kept as no-ops. `readLocalBlob(path)` reads via `localfile://`. |
| `notifications.js` | Pure helpers: `NOTIFICATION_CATEGORIES / VARIANTS / PRIORITIES`, `buildNotification`, `resolveDedupeStrategy`, `formatRelativeTime`, `storageKeyForUser`. |
| `notificationsRepo.js` | Supabase IO for the `notifications` table: `fetchRecent`, `insertOne(row, { ignoreDuplicates })` (upsert on `(user_id, dedupe_key)`), `deleteByDedupeKey`, `markRead`, `markAllRead`, `deleteOne`, `deleteAllForUser`, `subscribeForUser`. |
| `customRoles.js` | `listCustomRoles(projectId)`, `subscribeForProjectRoles`. Custom role = `base_role` + `custom_role_capabilities` overrides; resolution happens server-side via `has_capability()`. |
| `userStatus.js` | User status enum (online / away / dnd / offline) + `getStatusForUser`. |
| `recentProjects.js` | localStorage map of `projectId → lastAccessedAt` per `userId`. `markProjectAccessed`, `getMostRecentProjectId`, `getRecentMap`, `sortProjectsByRecent`. |
| `support.js` | `sendSupportReport({ category, title, body, screenshot? })` — fire-and-forget to `send-support-report` Edge Function. |
| `sendWelcome.js` | Fire-and-forget `send-welcome` Edge Function; no-op when already sent. |
| `plan.js` | `PLAN = { tier: 'Free', features: [...] }` placeholder — read in Account page AND Sidebar footer pill; update both when wiring real plans. |
| `platform.js` | Electron / web adapter: `isElectron`, `getAppVersion`, `isPackaged`, `showInFolder`, `openPath`, `onDeepLink`, `onAccountSwitch`, `openOAuthUrl`, `checkForUpdates`, `installUpdate`, `onUpdateStatus`, `showOSNotification`. Web stubs out anything that can't work in a browser. |
| `legalFeed.js` | Legal Newsfeed (Newsletter) data layer. `listLegalUpdates()` (embeds the user's `legal_update_states`), `setUpdateRead`/`setUpdatePinned`/`setUpdateSaved`, `getWeeklyDigest()` (invokes `legal-ai`'s `digest` action, cached 1 h in `sessionStorage`). |
| `ocr.js` / `transcribe.js` | Doc Viewer "Extract text" (Claude OCR) and audio/video captions (Whisper) — both call the `doc-ai` Edge Function. `transcribe.js` ships only the audio: for **video** it extracts the audio track in-renderer (Web Audio `decodeAudioData` → downmix + resample to 16 kHz mono → 16-bit PCM WAV; **no ffmpeg dep**) so the upload stays under Whisper's 25 MB cap (~13 min of speech). |
| `extractionHistory.js` | Per-file localStorage history of OCR snippets for the Doc Viewer (a *list* per file). |
| `captionsHistory.js` | Per-file localStorage cache of the audio pane's AI transcript — *one* result per file (text + timed segments + language), so reopening a file restores captions instantly instead of re-paying for Whisper. Key prefix `docvex:doc-viewer:captions:`. |

Other notable `lib/` modules (read source for depth):

| File | Purpose |
| --- | --- |
| `projectAi.js` | Client wrapper for the `project-ai` Edge Function + the `AI_MODELS` picker catalog. Backs the AI hub. |
| `documentGen.js` | AI document generation — builds `.docx`/`.pptx`/`.xlsx`/`.pdf` via the Anthropic Skills sandbox (Path A) with local builders (docx / pptxgenjs / SheetJS / jsPDF) as the offline fallback (Path B). |
| `docConstructor.js` | Pure model behind the Doc Viewer's **per-paragraph Constructor** (`components/DocConstructor`). Parses a generated document's line-based source into sections → pieces → `[[role.field]]` blanks, composes it back (`template` keeps blanks, `text` has them filled), fills a party from an identity record and settles gender / county-vs-sector / house-vs-flat clauses. There is no separate Constructor view any more: a `.docx` opens straight on the Word preview (`DocxWorkspace` → `DocxRenderPane` in DocViewer.jsx). Picking a paragraph ZOOMS the document in on it (a transform on the `.dv-docx` host; blur veil inside the host; ↑/↓, wheel and the corner buttons step between paragraphs), and `findPieceForText` ties it back to its source piece. When that piece has data (`pieceHasData`) the paragraph is shown through a PREVIEW COPY laid over the real one (`.dv-docx-livecard`, the component renders into it via a portal): every `[[blank]]` of the clause is an inline **input** in the sentence, and a panel docked under it lists the project's **identity records as a grid** (hover previews, click fills the party everywhere and rewrites the clause in the person/company formula; the former signature Sync/Restore was removed — the signature section is ordinary text). Those options sit UNDER THE PARAGRAPH they fill, in the pane's dock (`optionsSlot={null}`): the record being picked and the clause it rewrites are one thing to look at. They can be portalled elsewhere instead — `optionsSlot` ← the advisor context's `ctorSlot`, a node in `MultitoolComposer`, `.dv-advisor-ctorslot`, which collapses when empty — which is where they used to go. The per-paragraph ask field was removed either way; the side panel's composer is aimed at the picked paragraph. That dock (`.dv-docx-liftpanel`) lives OUTSIDE the zoomed host but rides the camera with the paragraph: `layoutLift` FLIPs it from under the card's current on-screen rect to its place on the host's own duration/easing (`.is-riding` — keep it in step with `.dv-docx`'s transition), and on close a lifeless clone of its markup (`.is-ghost`) flies back out. Leaving a paragraph puts the page back ON THAT PARAGRAPH (`exitParaRef` / `showExitPara`): the pane cannot scroll while it is zoomed (its overflow is off), so the scroll underneath is still where the document was when the FIRST paragraph was picked — after stepping through a few clauses, nowhere near the one being read. The index is remembered as the card lands, used once the zoom is out (centred in the pane, unless the paragraph is taller than it, which is pinned to its top), and used AGAIN by the next render swap (a save re-lays the pages, which may not break where they did) before being spent. The heading a paragraph sits under is NOT copied over it (a card showing "1. PĂRȚILE CONTRACTANTE" above clause 1.1 only repeated what the clause's own number says, and pushed the clause down the pane) — `.dv-docx-livehead` and its camera arithmetic are gone. Nothing reaches the document while a paragraph is open — changes show only in the copy and are written on exit. Such a paragraph is filled through its inputs, not typed over; one with no data stays editable in place. **A clause naming MORE THAN ONE PERSON colour-codes its blanks by person** (`personKeyOf` / `partyColor` / `partyColors` in DocConstructor): which gap belongs to the seller and which to the buyer is otherwise read off the prose around it. PERSON, not party — a company is named together with the human who signs for it ("SC X SRL, reprezentată legal prin Y, în calitate de administrator"), and that representative is a second person but NOT a second role: `representative` / `repCapacity` are fields of the company's own party (`REP_KEYS`, both `only: 'org'` in lib/identities). Grouping by role alone left exactly that clause — the commonest there is — with one colour and no way to tell the two people apart. A document DocVex did NOT write names nobody — its blanks resolve to a bare key (`addressBlock`, `regNo`) with `role: null` — so there the people are told apart by REPETITION: one entity cannot have two names, two CNPs or two registered offices, so a key appearing twice is the clause moving to the next entity (read in document order). Each person then gets a ROW of their own under the paragraph (`.dcx-people`, a rounded ground holding one `.dcx-person` per person: their colour, their name, `clauseKind` as **Persoană fizică / juridică**, and the records listed downwards) — a clause with two people needs two choices, and picking one writes only THAT person's blanks, and hovering previews them (`assignPerson` / `previewPerson`, by field id, since there is no role to fill by). A REPRESENTATIVE's row is filled by the record OF THAT PERSON, so the blank naming them (`representative`, a field of the COMPANY) takes the record's own name — `recordValueFor`; without it every value that row looked for was empty, so hover-preview and picking silently did nothing there while working everywhere else. The colour comes from the PERSON KEY'S NAME (djb2 over a palette of SIX, chosen by measurement: no two under 43° apart on the hue circle and each over 4.5:1 against white. The app's 12-colour avatar palette was wrong for this — three violets within 20°, two greens 2° apart, two pinks 2° apart — so two people could be given "different" colours nobody could tell apart), so a party keeps it between paragraphs — positional colours would swap over as soon as a clause named its parties the other way round — but two keys CAN hash to one colour, which in a clause about both is worse than no colour, so within a paragraph no colour is used twice: a clash walks on to the next free entry, and the palette is ordered so that walk lands on a distant hue. Verified across the real clause shapes — two parties, a company and its representative, two of each, five roles, six people — no repeats and nothing closer than 43°. Applied as a wash and an underline only: nothing that takes width, because the input's size is the one the document drew for the blank. A named swatch legend (`.dcx-legend`) goes with it — without a key the tints are decoration — A representative's row is labelled "· Representative" rather than as a party of its own. **Picking a record also SETTLES the picked clause** against what it says (`settleClauseFor` from `chooseRecord`): a house has no block/stair/floor/flat, so those blanks go together with the words introducing them (", bl. ___" whole), agreement follows the party's gender and "județul/sectorul" resolves to whichever Romania gives that address. Only the picked paragraph — a change you cannot see is not one you can check. Wiring this up exposed three bugs in `applyGenderToText` (lib/identities), all fixed: the pair list was missing the words an identification clause actually turns on (`Subsemnatul/Subsemnata`, `domiciliat/domiciliată`, `identificat`, `căsătorit`, `proprietar`…), so those kept their slashes; `Subsemnatul(a)` became `Subsemnatula` because the parenthetical was APPENDED when after `-ul` the feminine REPLACES it; and the optional trailing dot (there for `Dl./Dna.`) was eating the full stop that ended the sentence. **Versions are per paragraph**: `paragraphHistory` reads a paragraph's history out of the document versions and the pane shows it as a row of dots over the picked paragraph (click = put the paragraph back that way, as a draft change); the user's own saves no longer add a version card to the advisor thread (AI-written versions still do). The draft lives in `DocxWorkspace` (it outlives both the panel and the pane, which remounts on every rewrite); the panel's Save and closing the paragraph both go through the advisor's `saveConstructorVersion` as a new version. **Paragraph tools are not conditional on where the document was written**: a file with no versions of its own gets its source from the text read out of it, and its changes are written back into the .docx in place (`docxRewrite.js`), so picking, filling, typing over and per-paragraph history work the same on any Word document. |
| `identities.js` / `identityExtract.js` | **Identity records** — an ORGANISATION also carries the firm's **people** (`people: [{ id, role, name, nationalId, sharePct, shareValue, shares, link }]`, roles from `IDENTITY_PERSON_ROLES`): the shareholder table an act constitutiv states, shown in the viewer as a real table (`IdentityPeopleSection`) whose row either POINTS AT another record in the project (`link` = its filename, so the party's details stay in one place and open from there) or is typed in; amounts are kept AS TYPED (“30.000” — a float is how a capital figure goes wrong) and only the percentages are read as numbers, to total them and say so when they don't make 100. Contact lines a company publishes have their own fields (`phoneLandline`, `fax`, `website`; every telephone field gets the prefix control via `PHONE_KEYS`) plus `contacts: [{ id, label, value }]` for the open-ended ones (“Email departament financiar”). The table is also the ONE place saying who signs for the firm: the form’s former **Representation** section is gone, and `representative` / `repCapacity` — still record keys, because a clause fills them (“reprezentată legal prin [[vanzator.representative]]”) — are DERIVED from it by `settleRepresentative` (on every parse and on every edit of the table), taking the highest-ranking person who actually represents the company (`REP_ROLE_ORDER`: administrator, then director, then împuternicit… — a plain associate represents nobody) and their role as the capacity. A value already stated is never overwritten, and `IDENTITY_HIDDEN_KEYS` in DocViewer keeps the two keys from reappearing as stray rows at the foot of the form. The AI reader fills all of it: `autofillPrompt` asks an org document for `people` and `contacts` alongside the fields, and both arrive as READINGS with their own checkboxes rather than being written in — shown UNDER the field each one belongs to, drawn as the same accent pill as the banner over the sheet, and in that one place only (the Sources tab used to repeat the whole set as a second list of inputs, which meant two places to tick and no saying which had the last word). —  — a party to the case as a `.dvx` (JSON) file. Records are written BESIDE the documents (the folder being viewed, else the project root); nothing creates an `Identities/` folder any more — the Files tab's **Identities category** (Group by category) gathers them, and `listProjectIdentities` finds them anywhere (legacy `Identities/` folders still work). `identityExtract.js` reads ANY file to text (`readSourceText`: OCR for pictures and text-less PDFs, text layer / Office / plain-text extraction otherwise; audio, video and records themselves are refused) and backs both the viewer's multi-file "Fill from documents" picker (`readIdentityFromFiles`) and the Files right-click **Create identity** (`createIdentitiesFromFiles` — files are read together, so two sides of a card make one record and a contract makes one per party; merged into existing records, never overwritten). |
| `docxRewrite.js` | **Editing a Word file DocVex did not write.** Such a file has no source to rebuild from, and rebuilding it from the preview's text would throw away everything Word carries. So it is edited IN PLACE: the .docx is unzipped, `word/document.xml` (and any header/footer part) scanned for `<w:p>` elements, and only the paragraphs whose text matches an edit are rewritten — `rewriteDocxParagraphs(blob, [{before, after}])` → `{ blob, applied, missed }`. A rewritten paragraph keeps its `w:pPr` (style, numbering, indent, its table cell), its bookmarks/comment anchors and the FIRST run's `w:rPr`, which the new text inherits; formatting that varied INSIDE it (a bold word, a hyperlink) does not survive, since the new text is one run. Every other paragraph and part is byte-identical. `changedParagraphs` (lib/docConstructor) turns a Constructor draft into that edit list. Its `readDocxParagraphs(blob)` also supplies the SOURCE such a file is given — one line per `<w:p>` of `word/document.xml`, which is what the paragraph tools split into pieces. That source is parsed FLAT (`parseSource(src, { flat: true })`): no line is read as a heading or as the document's title, because a Word file's structure is in its styles, not its text, and a line read as a heading belongs to no piece and so cannot be picked. Reading the text off a RENDERED copy (`extractFileText` → `textContent`) is the trap this replaced: block elements contribute no line break, so the whole document arrived as one line, parsed as one enormous piece, and clicking any paragraph opened all of it. `findPieceForText` now also refuses a containment match below `LOOSE_MATCH_MIN` (0.6 length likeness), so an oversized piece can never swallow a paragraph again. TWO callers take the in-place path, which is what makes the paragraph tools unconditional: `DocxWorkspace.save` (a paragraph filled through its inputs) and the advisor's `applyManualEdit` → `writeParagraphsInPlace` (a paragraph typed over directly, which used to fail with `no_version`). Nothing in the UI distinguishes such a file any more — the note that explained the limitation was removed with the `foreign` prop on `DocConstructor`. |
| `docxSource.js` | **A DocVex Word file carries its own source.** The versions the paragraph tools work on (`text`, `template` with `[[blanks]]`, `values`, `assigned`, `manual`/`quiet`; an AI version's prompt is left out — the file is shared, the conversation isn't) are written INTO the `.docx` as a Custom XML part (`customXml/itemN.xml`, namespace `urn:docvex:document-source:v1`; Word keeps it on re-save, docx-preview ignores it) by `writeDoc` in DocViewer and by the project Advisor's `write_document`. On open, the advisor reads it (`readDocxSource`): no local record → the file's versions are used; the file disagrees with the record (rewritten elsewhere) → the file wins. A file written before this, opened on the device that HAS its record, gets the record written into it once — only when its text matches one of the versions (word-set likeness ≥ 0.6), which also picks the version that is on disk. Without it, a DocVex document opened on another computer lost its blanks, party assignments, paragraph picking and paragraph history. |
| `docThemes.js` | **Document themes** for the Word preview — `docvex` (the document as written), `Word Office`, `Chancery`: a palette + heading/body fonts each. Shown in the Doc Viewer's SIDE PANEL, not over the preview (the floating Word-style ribbon was removed): for a Word file `DocExtractPanel` adds **Theme** and **Add** tabs beside Advisor / Metadata (Theme = `DocThemeGrid`, a grid of thumbnails; Add is empty) and a **Quick actions** card above the side panel (`DocQuickActions`, portalled into `.dv-quick-card`: Page numbers + Open in Word, replacing the docx pane's old bottom-left button stack) — both exported from `components/DocRibbon.jsx`, styled like the app sidebar's tab buttons (selected theme = the pressed quick-action look). The state lives in `DocxRenderPane`, which publishes it to the panel through the advisor context as one memoised `docTools` object (memoised because publishing re-renders the provider and with it the pane). `applyDocTheme` stamps `data-doc-theme` + `--dt-*` vars on the `.dv-docx` host and DocViewer.css maps them onto docx-preview's style classes. PREVIEW ONLY (the file on disk isn't rewritten; Convert-to-PDF does carry it). A switch re-renders + re-paginates (fonts move lines), so it is locked while a paragraph is open or typed edits are unsaved. Remembered per file in `docvex:doc-viewer:doc-theme:<url>`. For Word files the find bar (`.dv-find`) sits in the pane's top-RIGHT corner, clear of the round Close button. |
| Word preview · paragraph affordances (in `pages/DocViewer.jsx`) | **`DocParaPill`** — every paragraph gets the app's morph pill (`useMorphPill`): a cursor-following tooltip naming the block ("Paragraph 1.1" / "Heading"), right-click morphs it into a dropdown with **Open** (the pick a click makes — `openPara`) and, on headings, **Collapse / Expand**. Fed by NATIVE listeners delegated on the host (docx-preview's nodes aren't React's) and isolated in its own component because the hook re-renders per mouse move; only a LEFT mousedown hides the tooltip — a right-click's mousedown precedes `contextmenu`, and the menu needs the tooltip's rect to morph out of. **Collapsible headings** (`docFoldLevel` / `markDocFolds` / `applyDocFolds`): Word's collapse triangle on styled headings AND paragraphs that read as one (short, all bold, numbered or capitals); folding hides everything down to the next heading of the same/higher level.
