A `Legi` record carries `TipAct`, `Numar`, `Titlu`, `Emitent`, `Publicatie`, `DataVigoare`, the act's FULL `Text`, and `LinkHtml` — the portal's own `/Public/DetaliiDocument/<id>` address, which is the only way to get one (there is no page addressable by number and year, and no `get by id` method — only `Search`). **The portal is a SOURCE, not a dependency.** Every answer is kept under `userData/legislation`: each result's metadata is folded into `archive.json` and an act that is opened is written whole to `acts/<id>.json`, so with the ministry's server down the same search runs against the index instead. **SPEED (2026-09-27): an act kept on this machine now OPENS FROM THAT COPY AT ONCE** (`loadAct`: the session's memory first — `actMemo` — then `acts/<id>.json`, then the portal; the page's background check still says "Live from the portal" or offers "Differs from the portal - click to sync"), its page from the copy (`fetchPage(rec, fresh)` is fresh only for an act just read live; `peekActPage` puts a page this session already has up in the same frame), live searches are kept for 10 minutes (`searchLegislation` memo; failures never kept), parsed page trees are kept (`parseActHtml` memo, last 8), and main keeps `archive.json` and the kept texts' size in memory (`legisIndexCache` / `legisBytesCache`). What follows describes the earlier rule — **An act OPENS FROM THE PORTAL FIRST** (`loadAct`: `fetchActLive`, kept whole on arrival; the copy on disk only when the portal cannot answer — offline, down, or the act gone — with `portalError` saying why; a record still carrying its text came from a live search a moment ago and counts as live) and so does its PAGE (`loadActPage` defaults to `fresh`; main's `legislation:page` falls back to `acts/<id>.html` itself when the fetch fails, and the lib asks for the copy a second time for a main process that predates that). What is on screen is therefore the law as it is today whenever the portal is up, and the copy is what keeps the tab working when it is not. The answer always says WHICH of the two it came from (`source: 'live' | 'archive'`) and the page shows it — the two are never silently mixed, because “no results” offline means something very different from the portal saying there are none. Quirks the model handles: a SOAP fault — an expired token ("TOKEN INVALID SAU EXPIRAT"; the portal invalidates a token when another client asks for one), a bad query — arrives as an HTTP **500** with the fault envelope in its body, so `legisPost` reads the body before judging the status and hands a fault back as an answer for the handler's one retry with a fresh token (treating the 500 as a transport failure kept the dead token in use for its ten-minute TTL, every search falling back to the archive; `courtsPost` reads its faults the same way); the text carries HTML ENTITIES of its own ("tatea &lt; 1,6 kg/cap" — a level the envelope's decoding never reaches; `unentity`, applied in `tidy`, on an act's text and on a page's preformatted rows — left in, a "&lt;" is four characters where a box-drawn table counted one and every row after it fell out of line, which is how half a table used to come through as text), `Titlu` is the printed act's whole masthead run together (BOM, tabs, the issuing body and gazette reference trailing the title — `cutTrailer`), `SearchAn` behaves like “in force that year” rather than “enacted that year” (so a typed year is applied client-side too: an act whose own year — read off its title — is known and differs is dropped; “Hotărâre 1383 2022” used to answer with the 2024 act of that number beside it; the archive search reads the year the same way), there is NO act-type parameter (so `LEGIS_TYPES` filters `TipAct` client-side, which is why a search can show fewer rows than the portal found), and matching is done on values folded to ASCII (`fold`) since Romanian is typed with ș/ț, with ş/ţ, or with neither. **The act is laid out from the PORTAL'S OWN PAGE, by the portal's own rules.** The service's `Text` is the act flattened; the page at `/Public/DetaliiDocument/<id>` marks the act's structure up with `S_*` classes — `S_DEN` (name), `S_HDR` (title), the `S_EMT` / `S_PUB` tables, `S_ART` › `S_ART_TTL` / `S_ART_DEN` / `S_ART_BDY` (an article), `S_ALN` (a numbered paragraph, "(1)"), `S_LIT` (a letter, "a)"), `S_PCT` (a point, "1."), `S_CIT` (text an amendment quotes), `S_NTA` (a note), `S_ANX` (an annex), `S_SMN` › `S_SMN_PAR` (the signatures), `S_PRE` › `S_PAR` rows (a preformatted block: a form, a model), `S_PAR` (a paragraph; nested in a body it is the portal's amendment note, "(la 16-11-2022, … a fost modificat de …)"), inline `S_LGI` (a reference) and `<A>` links, `TAG_COLLAPSED` (+/−) and `S_*_SHORT` (collapsed previews) to skip. `loadActPage(rec, { fresh })` gets the page (main's `legislation:page`) and `parseActHtml(html)` (DOMParser, renderer code; no `:scope` — found by walking children) reads it into a TREE — `{ head: { den, hdr, meta }, blocks, tables }`, a node being `{ kind, title, den, text, children }` for a unit (`art` / `aln` / `lit` / `pct` / `anx` / `cap` / `ttl` / `sec` … / `nta`), `{ kind: 'par', text, children }`, `{ kind: 'cit', children }`, `{ kind: 'smn', lines }`, `{ kind: 'pre', segs }` (runs of box rows → `{ type: 'table', id, rows, grid }`, the rest `{ type: 'text', rows }`); `actTreeStrings(tree, drawn)` lists every string in reading order (the find counts them; the marks are laid in the same order, so `renderNode` must visit title → den → text → lines/segs → children exactly as it does). **The HEAD is the portal's** (`.lg-src-head`, from `tree.head`): the act's name (`S_DEN`, its bracketed state — "(republicată)", "(*actualizată*)" — in bold, `denParts`), the title under it (`S_HDR`, or the first plain paragraph), then EMITENT and Publicat în as label / value rows in a fixed label column, as the portal's EMITENT table sets them; the record's own head (kind line, title, meta) is shown only until the page arrives. **Lists written as plain paragraphs** are read as lists (`asListItem`): an `S_PAR` opening with a marker — "a)", "1.", "(ii)", "-", "●" — or pushed in with spaces the TEXT carries (non-breaking spaces, or plain ones on its own line; whitespace with a line break or tab is the page's source formatting and does not count), never an amendment note, gets `title` (the marker), `list` (num / letter / roman / bullet / indent) and `indent` (0–4); drawn as `.lg-li`. **Nested lists hang on a GUIDE LINE**: `.lg-lit`, a point inside a paragraph, and the letter / roman / bullet / indent list items stand right of an accent hairline, spaced by PADDING so consecutive items keep one unbroken line, moved in further by `--li-indent`. Every number marker ("(1)", "a)", "1.") is in the theme's ACCENT, not the secondary text's blue-grey. The page renders the tree (`.lg-body.is-tree`: `.lg-art` with its label, `.lg-aln` / `.lg-lit` / `.lg-pct` as number-column rows indenting under each other, `.lg-cit` rule-marked, `.lg-amend` muted, `.lg-smn` right-aligned, `.lg-pre` › `.lg-preline` monospace rows + the tables) and falls back to the plain text's shape while the page is being fetched or cannot be had (offline with no copy). Verified under happy-dom on three saved pages (an order with 8 preformatted blocks / 17 tables / 3 annexes / 2 notes, a Government decision with 8 articles / 21 paragraphs / 10 letters / 9 points / 8 citations / signatures, an emergency ordinance). `parseActText` reads the act's SHAPE back off the plain text (and its BOX-DRAWN TABLES — older acts carry forms and schedules drawn in ┌─┬─┐ characters, which the portal flattens onto one line with runs of spaces where the row breaks were; `splitTables` takes the first row's width from `┌…┐` and cuts the rest into rows of that width, and `parseBoxTable` reads the drawing as a GRID — every column with a vertical/junction character is a column line, every row with `─` a row line, a cell grows right while the line through its band is not drawn and down while the line under it is not, its text is every row inside it (border rows included, a merged cell's words can sit on one) — `{ cols, rows: [[{ text, colSpan, rowSpan }]] }`, null for a drawing that is not a grid. The page draws each table with a SWITCH INSIDE its frame, in the TOP-RIGHT corner (`.lg-tblbar`, absolute in `.lg-tblwrap`; the table box, the drawing and the diagram keep a 38px band clear at the top for it) (`DocVex` · `Source`, the tab bar's toggle with the chosen one filled in the accent like the dice and the Search button; `drawnTables`, keyed `t:<id>` for a page table and by block index for a plain-text one): DocVex's real `<table class="lg-tbl">` with merged cells by default (in a `.lg-tblbox` frame), or the portal's drawing as a monospace `<pre class="lg-table">` where the boxes line up — both framed alike: the search bar's hairline, tint and frost, 8px corners (a collapsed table cannot round its corners, so the box carries the frame and each cell draws only its right and bottom hairlines, the table running a pixel past the box so the last ones fall under it); the switch itself has NO frost and no lift — the blur put its buttons on a compositing layer snapped to whole pixels while the act's text sits on fractions, and the words inside jumped a pixel as the page scrolled; the find counts whichever is shown. NO LINE BREAKS INSIDE A WORD in either: the drawing never wraps (it scrolls sideways), and DocVex's cells wrap only after a word (`overflow-wrap: normal; word-break: keep-all`), a word the drawing hyphenated across two rows ("repro-" / "ducţie") being made whole again when the cell's rows are joined. A cell's rows become its LINES (`lines` beside `text`): a row opening an item — a bullet (● • - –), a number ("1."), a letter ("a)") — starts a line, a blank row ends one, the rest continues the last; the page draws each line on its own (`.lg-tbl-line`), so the eligibility box's six bullets stand one under the other instead of running together. Verified on Ordinul 228/2012's APIA forms: 17 tables, 485 rows, every row the same width, all 17 read as grids) **DIAGRAMS** — an organisation chart drawn in the same characters is NOT a grid, and the grid reader answered it with nonsense, so a run of box rows is tried as a diagram FIRST (`parseBoxDiagram`): a box is a `┌` whose top edge runs along `─`/junctions to a `┐`, whose left edge runs down `│`/junctions to a `└`, with the `┘` and the other two edges drawn; two or more boxes side by side or one inside another make a diagram; a box holding another is a FRAME; every other line character is a CONNECTOR, each character saying which of its four sides it joins (`DIR_OF`) and a junction on a box's border drawing only the side pointing away from the box, the half-segments merged into runs — `{ w, h, boxes: [{ x, y, w, h, lines, frame }], hseg, vseg }` in character units. The page draws it as an SVG chart (`BoxDiagram`, `.lg-diagram`: a cell is 8 × 17 px, a box a rounded card at its corner cells' centres, a frame a dashed outline, connectors a muted stroke, the words real HTML in a foreignObject so the find marks them; the chart scales down to the column, never up), Source being the drawing as ever; `diagramStrings` lists its words for the find. Verified on HG 725/2010's MADR chart: 27 boxes, 1 frame, 33 line runs; the order's 17 tables still read as grids. In the PLAIN-TEXT fallback a chart is still cut by `splitTables` at the first box's width (the flattened text has no row width to go by), so it reads as text until the page arrives. — the portal puts each structural unit on a line of its own separated by a bare `+`, so divisions, articles and their numbered paragraphs come back as blocks and the act is laid out as a document rather than dumped as a wall of characters (verified on the consolidated Legea nr. 24/2000: 69,912 characters → 14 units, 85 articles, 245 paragraphs, none empty). **Nothing of the portal's own design is copied** — the page is built from the app's semantic tokens, which is both the house rule and what keeps this clear of anyone's trade dress; what is carried across is the text, which carries no copyright (Legea nr. 8/1996 art. 9) and whose reuse the portal's terms allow. Every act shows the portal's own caveat, on the act and not in an About page: a consolidated text is NOT authentic, only the Monitorul Oficial print is. **References in an act behave as in the Doc Viewer** (`LegalRefPill` in pages/Legislation.jsx, one morph pill for the page, native listeners delegated on it; each `.lg-ref` carries `data-ref-hit` → its lib/lawRefs hit): hovering shows the viewer's HIGHLIGHT PILL and a click EXPANDS it into the CARD — `components/RefHitPill` `refHitPill(hit, { full })`, the viewer's `refPill` built from a hit instead of a marked span (RefPill.css) — whose **Search** does what the click used to (`onRef`: the act here, the CAEN modal, Court files, ANAF) and Close; Enter on a focused reference still follows it. The APP SIDEBAR's Legislation tab rows show the same pill on hover (`legalTabPill` in Sidebar.jsx: the platform's dot and site, the loaded name, the kind, what a click does). **THE TABS ANSWER EACH OTHER — the references in an act are CONTROLS** (`marked` in Legislation.jsx, over the same detector as the Doc Viewer's: `findFollowableRefs` in lib/lawRefs = the `act` / `code` / `caen` hits of `findLawRefs` + `findCaseRefs`, court file numbers — "Dosarul nr. 1.234/1/2022", the word required, thousands dots dropped — found once per string and cached per act): a cited ACT (`.lg-ref`, legislatie.just.ro’s violet `--cat-update`) is FOLLOWED HERE — but ONLY a citation that fills the form WHOLE: kind, number AND year (`fixedQueryOf`: `lawRefDetails` → `legislationQueryFor`, all three present — "Legea nr. 287/2009", "art. 5 din O.U.G. nr. 195/2002"; "Codul muncii" or a directive the portal has no kind for is left as plain text, since it could only be found by WORDS, and the words search with the AI behind it is the user's to run, never a click's); `followCitation` fills Kind / Number / Year with it, runs that FIXED search (no title words, no AI) and opens the act when the answer is one act (`openIfUnambiguous`, shared with the `?open=1` arrival), else shows the results; a CAEN code (`.is-caen`, insse.ro’s amber) opens the nomenclature in a MODAL over the page — **`components/CaenModal`** (`{ code, codes?, rev? }`: the code in its words box and its card open at once, the same search as the CAEN tab's down the left, the codes a citation listed as chips, "Open in the CAEN tab" the way to the tab itself; the card is **`components/CaenCard`**, the CAEN tab's own entry card moved out so any tab can show it — `cn-` styles, Caen.css); a COURT FILE number (`.is-case`, portal.just.ro’s blue `--info`; a CUI `.is-cui`, anaf.ro’s green) opens the Court files tab on that file (`/portal-just?nr=`). **The rail is ALWAYS there, and the SEARCH lives in its Search item**: before anything is asked the Search item is the tabs' EMPTY STATE (`.lg-start`: the placeholder tabs' `.lss-card` — a bare thin-stroke mark, a title, a muted line, centred) holding the two ways to search STACKED in one centred column (by number, "or", by words) at a LARGER size (the bar's and the box's tokens re-declared in `.lg-start-mode`: 36px fields, 13.6px type) with "or" between them on the fields' line, each labelled and hinted — BY NUMBER (the bar) and BY WORDS (the words box) — and Clear under them; once there is an answer the same fields stand as one compact row (`.lg-searchrow`) over it; the RESULTS SECTION is for a WORDS search — a search by the form alone (typed or filled by a citation) whose answer is one act, or versions of one, OPENS it and lists nothing (`runNow` returns `opened`, which `openIfUnambiguous` respects), and only an answer of several different acts (or none) is listed; a fixed search with words too is opened and listed; a **Clear** button in the search row empties the form, the words and the answer (and drops a search still on its way); **SEARCH is the mini header's**: at the far LEFT of its second line (the page's `tools`), a control the SAME SIZE as the act's find field (`.lg-searchtab`: the find's height, width, hairline, corners, tint and frost; icon and word centred; its HOVER and SELECTED looks the APP SIDEBAR's, as the rail's items wear them — a pointer-tracked wash on hover; selected while the search is on show: Ink accent tint, ring and text, Cream the lifted cognac pill — at rest its field look), with History (the clock alone, `HistoryButton` `iconOnly`, `.lg-searchtab-hist`) beside it; the rail holds only the acts opened; Search + the line's gap + History = the rail's width (`--lg-rail-w`, 236px), so the pair ENDS ON THE RAIL'S DIVIDER; the rail's items wear the APP SIDEBAR's look (`.lg-rail-item` = `.nav-item`, components/Sidebar.css — keep them in step): no icon, TWO LINES — the act's kind on top in small capitals (`.lg-rail-kind`, "ORDONANȚĂ DE URGENȚĂ"), its number and year under it (`.lg-rail-num`, "nr. 64/2012"), a 44px row — the same weight and size selected or not; HOVER a wash brightening where the pointer is (accent on Ink, neutral on Cream; `--item-spot-x/y` from `useItemSpots`), SELECTED the sidebar's selected tab (Ink: accent tint, ring and text with a brighter pointer glow on hover; Cream: the solid cognac pill, lifted, cream text); the rail itself carries the sidebar's SPOTLIGHT — a soft accent glow following the pointer (`::before`) and, once pinned, a shine on its border (`::after`), both from `--spot-x/y` eased toward the pointer by Sidebar.jsx's frame-rate-independent loop; the DIVIDER between the rail and the act is a DRAG HANDLE (`.lg-rail-resizer`, a band on the rail's right border that lights in the accent): dragging sets `--lg-rail-w` on the page (180–420px, kept per device in `docvex:legislation:rail-w`; double-click = 236px; ←/→ in 16px steps when focused), and since the Search button reads the same width the pair above still ends on the divider; the act's column has a 20px RIGHT padding, the same as the gap between the divider and it; the rail's LEFT EDGE is the mini header's (pulled left exactly as the bar is — `--lg-rail-pull` = the content gap less the window-edge inset — and widened by the same, its items padded back onto the content edge); the rail runs from just under the mini header to the BOTTOM of the window (`--lg-bar-h` / `--lg-scroll-h`, measured with a ResizeObserver); it is STICKY under the header, NOTHING sits below the shell (the page's and the scroller's bottom padding moved into `.lg-shell-main` — any space under the shell let the sticky rail be pushed up at the end of the scroll), and the shell is kept at least the rail's height plus the distance the page scrolls before it sticks (`--lg-rail-travel`, measured) — without that, on the Search screen or a short act the block around the rail was no taller than the rail and the rail scrolled away (making it `position: fixed` instead was tried and broke the layout: an ancestor here makes its own frame, so "fixed" is measured from the wrong box); its surface is SYNCED to the header's — LegalTabs hands its own pinned state over (`onPinnedChange`, a layout effect, so the rail repaints on the very frame the bar does) and the rail renders `is-pinned` from it — and, pinned, takes the mini header's pinned surface — the frosted page ground, the flat hairline all round, the bar's corners — and, pinned, rises to z 20, above the page's bottom fade (`.sv-single-body::after`, z 5) and below the bar (z 30), its height gliding to end one `--chrome-inset` above the window's bottom so its rounded foot floats — (NOT the seam shadow: it masks the gap above the bar at the window's top, and on the rail it painted a strip of backdrop colour between bar and rail) (one border all round, only its right edge showing until then, so nothing moves when the frame appears); the search view opens with `.lg-searchrow` — the bar (dice, Kind, Number, Year, Search) and the words box (`LegalSearchBox`, exported from LegalTabs: the tab bar's search box as a component of its own, with its Ctrl/⌘+F), then the bar's note; the tab bar's second line carries only the OPEN act's FIND, at its right end, which which behaves as the Doc Viewer's find bar (and Windows') does — every match lit as the words are typed, the position ("3/17" / "No results" in red) and previous / next / clear INSIDE the field (`search.find` = `{ current, total, prev, next }` on `LegalSearchBox`, `.lgt-find-count` / `.lgt-find-btn`), Enter the next match, Shift+Enter the one before, Escape clears and leaves the field and, with no act open, no box at all (LegalTabs' `noSearch`: the box stays LAID OUT but hidden and inert, `.lgt-search.is-hidden`, so the line keeps its height and nothing moves when an act is opened and the find box comes back). **READING SESSIONS, in a RAIL** (`tabs` / `activeTab` in Legislation.jsx, kept in page memory): the Advisor's list of chats, for acts — every act opened from the results, from History or from a citation arriving from the Doc Viewer starts a SESSION that stands as an item down the page's left (`.lg-rail`, sticky under the tab bar, `.lg-rail-item` = `.aichat-item` to the letter, keep them in step; the way back to the search and its results is the mini header's Search control, lit when no session is active); the active session's reading state IS the live state (act, page, find, table switches — NO scroll position: moving between items, or opening one, always lands at the TOP of the page, and the restored find does not pull it down to its match until the find is used again, `skipFindScroll`) and the others hold theirs as snapshots (`stashActive` / `applyReader` / `showTab`; `leaveToResults` puts the active one away and shows the search — Back with no trail, Search pressed, a search submitted, a History search picked; `closeTab` removes one); an act already open in a session is switched to, not opened twice; a citation followed INSIDE an act opens as a NEW session (`followCitation`: the query run, the act opened when unambiguous, else the results shown), the act it was cited in staying open in its own; an answer arriving after the reader moved to another session lands in its own item. `open` is remade every render (it reads the sessions), so `runNow` and `openIfUnambiguous` call it through `openRef`. With no session the page is the search and its results, as it was. There is NO Back button: the rail's Search item is the way back to the search (the act keeps its place in the rail). Background layers only, no padding, no font change, so marking cannot re-wrap a line. |
| `courts.js` | **Court files, from portal.just.ro** (`pages/PortalJust`, `/portal-just`). The courts' free SOAP service (`portalquery.just.ro/query.asmx`, called from main — `courts:*`) answers a file WHOLE: number, court, section, object, category / stage, every party with its role, every hearing with the solution and its summary, the appeals. `searchCases({ numar, parte, obiect, institutie, from, to })`, `listHearings({ institutie, day })`, `COURTS` (+ `courtLabel`, `KIND_LABEL`) from `courts.json` — the service's `Institutie` enumeration, ids as it spells them ("JudecatoriaSECTORUL4BUCURESTI"), rebuilt by `scripts/build-courts.mjs`; `nextHearing` / `lastSolution` / `partiesByRole` / `casesSorted` / `fmtDate`. A party-name search can answer hundreds of files; main caps at 400 and reports the total. `?nr=1234/3/2026` opens straight on a file. Nothing kept on disk (a file's state is what the court says today). `?parte=<name>` searches a party's name on arrival — ANAF's "Court files" button sends a company's name here. |
| `anaf.js` | **A company's fiscal record, from ANAF** (`pages/Anaf`, `/anaf`). ANAF's public REST service (`webservicesp.anaf.ro/api/PlatitorTvaRest/v9/tva`, called from main — `anaf:lookup`; free, no key, one request a second, 100 CUIs per request): `parseCuis(text)` (RO prefix and punctuation dropped, unique, ≤100), `lookupCompanies(text)` → `{ asOf, companies, notFound }`, `normalizeCompany` flattening `date_generale` / `inregistrare_scop_Tva` / `inregistrare_RTVAI` / `stare_inactiv` / `inregistrare_SplitTVA` / the two addresses into one record (cedilla ş/ţ rewritten). The page shows flags (VAT payer, VAT on collection, inactive, struck off, split VAT, e-Factura), the CAEN code opens the nomenclature in the CAEN modal (`components/CaenModal`, with "Open in the CAEN tab" inside it), a **Court files** button beside Copy searches the courts' portal for files naming the company (`/portal-just?parte=`), Copy (an icon-only button, the word removed) writes the party as a contract names it. `?cui=…` looks up on arrival. The record's facts, in order: Registered office (with a **Map** pill that slides `components/MapDrawer` in from the right — Google Maps' keyless embed `maps?q=…&output=embed` pinned on the address, "Google Maps" to open the site; the packaged CSP carries `frame-src https://www.google.com https://maps.google.com`), CUI, Trade register, Age (`ageOf`), Founded (`longDate` of ANAF's `data_inregistrare`), Main CAEN, **CAEN for the last financial year** (`companyBilant` → main's `anaf:bilant`, ANAF's free `webservicesp.anaf.ro/bilant?an=&cui=` — the code declared in the balance sheet, which can differ from the registered one; caen 0 = no statement filed; cached per session), EUID (`euidOf`: `ROONRC.` + the trade-register number), then registration, VAT and the rest. |
| `caen.js` | **The CAEN nomenclature, bundled — ALL THREE REVISIONS.** `caenRev3.json` (~150 KB: every section / division / group / class of Rev. 3 + each Rev. 2 class and the Rev. 3 classes it went to; built by `scripts/build-caen.mjs` from the INS xlsx files), `caenRev2.json` (~85 KB, the whole Rev. 2 tree) and `caenRev1.json` (~80 KB, the whole Rev. 1 tree + `to2`, the INS Rev. 1 → Rev. 2 correspondence) — the last two built by `scripts/build-caen-old.mjs` from the INS PDFs and the annex of Ordinul INS nr. 601/2002 (read off legislatie.just.ro's service; the INS no longer publishes Rev. 1 itself) — and `caenRev3Notes.json` (~780 KB: the INS explanatory notes for Rev. 3 — includes / also includes / excludes; the older revisions have no notes bundled). Every tree has ONE shape, `{ rev, source, sections, items: { code: { l, n, p } } }`, so the page, the tree, the picker and the card read any revision alike. Lazy-imported: `loadCaen` (Rev. 3), `loadCaenRev(rev)`, `loadCaenNotes`; `REV_INFO` (years in force + the act, per revision), `REVS_ALL`; `caenNext(trees, rev, code)` / `caenPrev(trees, rev, code)` — where a class went in the next revision / came from in the one before, read across the loaded trees. NOT fetched at runtime: CAEN changes by whole revisions years apart, so a new revision = re-run a script + ship a release. **Two revisions are live at once**: documents before 2025 cite Rev. 2, and a number can mean a different activity in each (`6201` = custom software in Rev. 2; Rev. 3 moved it to `6210`). `resolveCaen(data, code, rev)` reads a citation in the revision it names (lawRefs' `caen` hits now carry `rev`) and otherwise in Rev. 3, with `changed` set when the Rev. 2 class went to a DIFFERENT number (a reworded name is not a change). Also `searchCaen(data, q)` (ONE tree, code prefix or folded words, every hit tagged with the tree's `rev`; the page's 'All revs' runs it over each tree and joins the answers), `caenEntry` / `caenChildren` / `caenFromRev2` / `caenRev2Entry`, `noteLines` (links the codes after “vezi”), `caenHref` (`/caen?code=…&rev=2`). |
| `exportPdf.js` | Office-preview → PDF (lazy `html2canvas` + `jspdf`), one page per rendered element. |
| `conversationHistory.js` | Per-file store of the Doc Viewer AI-advisor thread + generated-doc iterations (the project index's PRIVATE store per user, localStorage `docvex:doc-viewer:conversation:<path>` as the fallback). The viewer WAITS for this user's conversations to be in memory before reading a file's thread (`conversationsSettled` / `whenConversationsReady`, awaited by the provider's load effect) — read too early, a file opened on an empty thread and the next message saved over its history. |
| `mail.js` | Gmail/Outlook OAuth client for the Mail tab (`mail-sync` / `mail-callback`), three-leg PKCE-style flow. |
| `admin.js` | `get_admin_stats` aggregates + admin-allowlist CRUD (`app_admins`). |
| `appZoom.js` / `appScale.js` | `appZoom.js`: base-zoom constant (now **1**) + `toLayoutPx` viewport→layout-px helper. `appScale.js`: normalises the Settings display-scale preference (70–125%, 5% steps). |
| `audioEnvelopeCache.js` / `captionPosition.js` | Doc Viewer audio: cached waveform envelope (LRU, base64 in localStorage) and the persisted caption-overlay position. |
| `chat.js` / `privateMessages.js` | Supabase IO for `chat_messages` (+ reactions, threads, pins) and `private_messages` (DMs). |
| `extractFileText.js` | Best-effort text extraction (txt/PDF/DOCX/XLSX) for AI attachments. |
| `useChatFind.js` | Find-in-conversation via the CSS Custom Highlight API (no DOM mutation). |
| `fileDragBus.js` / `folderColors.js` / `projectsDir.js` / `projectFilesPrefetch.js` | Files-page support: rich drag-preview bus; per-folder colour tags; per-user "projects folder" pref; background Files warm-cache (Electron-only). |
| `thumbnailDescriptor.js` | Descriptor builders (`describeLocalFile` / `describeLooseFile`) feeding `thumbnailEngine.js`. |
| `whatsappChat.js` | WhatsApp `.txt` export parser/detector (Android + iOS layouts). |
| `aiFileIndex.js` | One-time per-file AI description (Haiku, batched 8 text / 4 images), cached in `localStorage` (`docvex:ai-file-index:v1`) against size+mtime. Makes AI search cheap: a photo is decoded and uploaded once in its life, not once per search. |
| `aiSearchCache.js` | AI-search answer cache — `(folder signature, normalised query) → hits` in `docvex:ai-search-answers:v1`. A repeat search costs nothing; any add/delete/rename/edit changes the signature and invalidates the folder's answers. |
| `projectSync.js` | **Sync a project to the signed-in account** (Project → Overview → "Sync with account"). Mirrors the project's local folder into a private Storage bucket so another device signed into the same account can pull the same working set — NOT a return to the cloud file store migration 031 removed: the folder on disk stays authoritative, and a project is only copied up once someone switches the toggle on for it. **No tables** (migration 035 adds only the bucket): everything lives in `project-sync/<project id>/` — a MANIFEST (`.docvex-sync.json`: `{ files: { [key]: { path, size, mtime } } }`) whose existence IS the toggle, plus one object per file. An object's key is a HASH of the file's path inside the project, never the path itself (Romanian case files are full of spaces, diacritics and brackets; the manifest maps key → path, the only direction anything needs). One `list('')` call tells a fresh device which of the account's projects have a copy waiting — which is what puts a **cloud mark** on a Hub row (`countLocalFiles` = 0 on this machine → `.lh-cloud-mark` + a "Get files" button, desktop only: the web build has no per-project folder to compare against). The newer copy wins, by modified time (±2s slack for filesystem rounding), and the third leg of the comparison is a per-device localStorage LEDGER of what this machine last had synced — without it a file in the manifest and not on disk is indistinguishable from one a teammate has just added, and sync would either lose the deletion or resurrect the file. 50 MB per file (the bucket's own cap too); bigger ones are reported as skipped, never silently dropped. `syncStatus` / `planSync` back the panel's state line without doing anything. **The project's DATA syncs too** (`lib/projectSyncData.js`, run at the end of every sync): everything kept in localStorage ABOUT the files — AI data, metadata, captions, OCR snippets, `aiFileIndex` descriptions, timeline scan readings, document themes, folder colours, the case timeline — goes in a SHARED bundle `<project id>/.docvex-data.json`, and the user's own conversations (Doc Viewer advisor threads, Advisor chats) in a PRIVATE one, `user-<user id>/<project id>.json` (migration 036 opens that folder to its owner only; before it, the upload is refused and the panel says so). Files are named by their path inside the project; size+mtime stamps are rewritten to the manifest's version going up and to the local stat coming down. Newest wins per entry — stores with no timestamp of their own are dated by `lib/syncClock.js` (`setIfChanged` / `removeAndTouch`: touched only on a REAL change), AI data merges per facet, OCR snippets and chats per item, deleted chats leave tombstones (`markGone`). A pulled file keeps its modified time (`write-tree` sets it via `utimes`). Waveform envelopes sync too (8-bit, re-indexed into the cache's LRU). NOT synced: AI-search answers (keyed by a hash of absolute paths), device UI prefs, the Trash's contents, and the `.docvex.json` sidecar (dotfiles are never listed; its ids are device-local). **A new per-file or per-project store must be added to `FILE_STORES` / `PROJECT_STORES` there, or it stays on the device.** **The FILE sync is three-way** (manifest × disk × per-device ledger `{ gen, files: { key: mtime }, folders }`; `gen` = the manifest's generation, so a ledger from a copy that was switched off and on again is ignored): new/changed → up or down, deleted here → removed from the account, deleted ELSEWHERE → moved to this device's Trash (never hard-deleted; a file changed here since the last sync is kept and sent back up instead). FOLDERS are in the manifest too (`folders: { rel: 1 }`, listed by `list-recursive`'s `dirs`), so empty folders exist everywhere; a folder deleted elsewhere is removed here only if empty (`local-folder:remove-empty-dirs`, `rmdir`). Files over 45 MB go up in parts (`<key>.p0…`, `parts` in the manifest; bucket cap is 50 MB per object), up to `MAX_SYNC_FILE_BYTES` (2 GB — the renderer holds a file whole). Pulled files are written one at a time. Sync is MANUAL (Overview → Sync now / the Hub's Get files). **App version gate:** every sync stamps the manifest with the app's version (`appVersion`, never lowered); an app OLDER than the stamp may neither sync the project (`syncProject` refuses, `needsVersion`) nor open it — `components/ProjectVersionGate` wraps every project-scoped route in AppRoutes (`/projects/:id`, files, chat, events, ai…; not Mail/Playbook) and shows an "Update Docvex to open this project" screen. A NEWER app is let in and its next sync raises the stamp (requiring EQUAL versions would deadlock: nobody can install an older build). The last stamp is remembered per device (`docvex:sync:app-version:<id>`, `-` = checked, nothing to honour) so the gate answers at once, holds offline, and is re-read from the account in the background and on window focus; with nothing remembered it waits ≤4 s, then lets the project open (offline must not lock anyone out). Version compare: `lib/appVersion.js`. |
| `docRules.js` | **Document rules — the STATED half of the Playbook.** `writingStyle.js` beside it learns how a person writes from their own documents; this is what cannot be learned: how a document is NUMBERED and laid out. A model asked twice answers "Art. 1" once and "CAPITOLUL I" the next time, so consistency between two documents from one office has to be a decision, not an inference. `RULE_GROUPS` is the catalogue — section heading (`Art. 1` / `CAPITOLUL I` / `Secțiunea 1` / `1.` / `I.` / none), heading case, clause numbering (`1.1.` / `1.1` / `(1)` / `1.` / none), sub-point marker (`a)` / `a.` / `(a)` / `i)` / `-` / `•`), the level below it, **empty fields** (`_____` by default, or `.........` / `[_____]` / `[.........]` — all UNNAMED: the rule says what the AI WRITES, and the design is an unmistakable gap plus a second pass that works out what belongs in it. A bracketed gap is the surest, needing no judging at all. Named tokens are no longer offered here but are still RECOGNISED everywhere — a named token is the only shape that is not merely found but UNDERSTOOD, since `parseFieldToken` reads `[[vanzator.legalName]]` as a party's field for an identity record to fill), plain-list bullet, whether numbering restarts per section, named clauses, then dates, amounts, party naming, defined terms — plus a free-text `extra`. There is NO language rule (removed at the user's request): every document is written in the language of the request (`LANGUAGE_RULE`, always in the steer), and the example and samples are shown in Romanian. The choices are deliberately the shapes `MARKER_RE` / `headingOf` in `docConstructor.js` recognise, so a document written to these rules still reads back correctly in the paragraph tools. `rulesOutline` renders the settings as an example document (the page's preview: numbering rules are unreadable as prose and obvious as a skeleton) and `buildDocRulesSteer` turns them into the instruction block that rides on every drafting turn — appended beside the writing-style block by `withStyleSteer` (writingStyle.js) and by `generateDocument` (projectAi.js), so both call paths carry both. Defaults are what the drafter did before the page existed. **A blank is a CHARACTER choice, never a font one** (there is a note at the foot of the file): the model is handed text, so the font is stripped before it ever sees it and cannot make a blank more detectable — and a bespoke face would not survive the file anyway, since Word renders a font only when it is installed or embedded and substitutes it otherwise. **EVERY choice is detected by the app, not just DocVex's own token, and by ONE detector.** `BLANK_PATTERNS` / `looksLikeBlank` / `scanBlanks` / `replaceFields` / `stripBlanks` in `docConstructor.js` are now the single home for what a blank looks like — the Word preview's chips (`pages/DocViewer`) import them instead of keeping the second, longer list they used to have. The two disagreed: the preview drew a chip on `[Client name]`, `{{x}}`, `{x}`, `<x>`, a dotted rule and `……` that the Constructor could not fill, so a blank you could see was not always one you could answer. Ten shapes, one judgement (a rule of underscores or dots always counts; a delimited shape needs a letter inside it, so `[3]` and `[2020]` are left alone; `{ margin: 0 }` is a CSS rule, not a blank), longest match wins on overlap — a named shape (`[[nume]]`, `⟨nume⟩`) becomes a field of that name, an unnamed one (`_____`, `[_____]`) is called after where it sits. `fillText`'s `keep` hands the blank back EXACTLY as the document writes it, which is what lets a foreign file still be found by its own text when saved (verified: `before` matches the file byte for byte for all four). A line that is nothing but underscores is a horizontal rule and `parseSource` drops it first; markdown `__bold__` is two underscores and does not match. **Word's own blanks** are found too (`drawnBlanks` in DocViewer): a template does not write "[name]", it draws a LINE — a run of spaces or tabs with underline on — which is the most obvious blank there is to a reader and pure whitespace to every text pattern. They are found in the RENDERED document (docx-preview puts `w:u` on the run's span as `text-decoration: underline`), never in the file's text, because a marker written into the text would make the paragraph unrecognisable to `findPieceForText` and break paragraph picking. The run is WRAPPED, not rewritten — its spaces stay, so the line keeps the width pagination measured for it, `dvfieldRaw` puts the line back when the blank is cleared, and `clearDocFields` only takes the mark off (unwrapping would throw away the underline). Their ids are `d1…` against `walkDocFields`' `f1…`. Likewise every NUMBERING choice is recognised: `HEAD_SRC` was added to `MARKER_RE` so `Art. 1` / `CAPITOLUL I` / `Secțiunea 1` / `I.` carry their number even where headings are not read as headings (a foreign document, parsed flat, previously had numberless articles), and `headingOf` now accepts a short unpunctuated ALL-CAPS line as a section, which is what the "No number" rule produces — the FIRST such line is still the document's title (`bare` on the heading tells the caller which). **Presets** (`loadRulePresets` / `addRulePreset` / `updateRulePreset` / `removeRulePreset` / `presetMatches` / `nextPresetName`, `docvex.docRules.presets.v1`, ≤40): named sets of the rules (the in-use switch excluded). The Playbook's Document rules section is a RAIL + DETAIL split (the Legislation workspace's shape, its `.lg-rail-item`s): the rail lists every preset — ONE marked "· in use": the one last put in use (`loadActivePresetId` / `saveActivePresetId`, `docvex.docRules.activePreset.v1`, default DocVex; `presetInUse` = that one while it still matches the rules, else the first that does), so a new preset (a copy of the rules in use) is NOT in use until Use these rules (editing it, or resetting it, changes the rules in use with it); only when the rules in use match NO preset do they get an item of their own at the top, "Custom rules" — — **Default** first, BUILT IN (`BUILTIN_PRESET_ID` — id still `docvex`, `builtin: true`: always present, never deleted or renamed, but EDITABLE — its rules start as `DEFAULT_RULES`, are kept under its id like any preset's, and Reset puts them back — the Reset button is always laid out and only HIDDEN while there is nothing to reset, so the In use pill beside it never moves; the read-only mode it had was removed at the user's request); a new preset is named "Custom rules", "Custom rules 2"… by `nextPresetName`) — with a **+** at its top (a new preset = a copy of the rules in use, opened with its name selected); the right side shows the selected item's rules, EDITABLE (a preset's edits save into the preset), its preview, and its actions — for a preset: rename in place (the title is the field), Use these rules (or an "In use" pill when it matches), Delete (pressed twice; also × on the rail item); for the live rules: Reset and the In use / Paused switch. The writing-voice IMPORT (the DropZone, its reading rows and the imported documents with their remove buttons — the page's former "Documents you wrote" section) now stands INSIDE a custom preset, under its header (`RulesSection`'s `importSlot`, `.pbk-pimport`), with the learned voice ("Your writing voice", once there is one) under it — so the rules section is the LAST thing on the page. **The page scrolls in ONE PANE of its own** — on this page the app's page scroller is switched off (`.sv-single-scroll:has(.pbk-rules-split)` → `overflow: hidden`) and `.pbk-rules-split` fills it (`position: absolute; inset: 0; overflow-y: auto; container-type: size`): the masthead (`.pbk-phead`, passed into `RulesSection` as `head`), then `.pbk-pgrid` — the presets list left, the settings (`.pbk-pmain`) right. The masthead and settings scroll; the list (`.pbk-prail`) is `position: sticky`, starting 12px under the masthead's divider and holding one inset under the pane's top once it has scrolled away, its FOOT always one inset above the bottom of the screen, the app sidebar's rule: stuck, `height: calc(100cqh - 2 × inset)` (the pane is a size container); before it sticks its height is a SCROLL-DRIVEN animation (`pbk-rail-grow`, `animation-timeline: scroll(nearest block)`, range 0 → header + 8px − inset) from the pane less the header, the gap and the inset, to the stuck height — no script on scroll; the one measurement is the header's height (`--pbk-head`, ResizeObserver on `.pbk-phead`), and `.pbk-pgrid`'s min-height is the grown list's footprint so the list's height never changes the page's length, its ITEMS scrolling inside it (`.pbk-prail-list`), heading and + staying put; the SELECTED preset is sticky inside the list (`top: 0; bottom: 0` — held at the list's top or foot when scrolled out of view; on Ink its accent tint is laid over `--bg-page` so passing items don't show through). It cannot be dragged at the end because nothing follows the grid in the pane and the pane has no bottom padding (the air is the detail's 64px). It is drawn as the APP SIDEBAR by `.sidebar`'s own rules (keep them in step with components/Sidebar.css): frosted page ground (Cream: `--bg-sidebar` 90%), 1.5px `--border` ring, 9.6px corners, `--shadow-card`, 12.8×9.6px padding and the pointer spotlight (glow + border shine from `--spot-x/y`, eased by Sidebar.jsx's loop, copied into RulesSection). SPACING is the Legislation tab's (keep in step with LegalWorkspace.css / LegalTabs.css): the header's divider has its left end pulled out to one `--rail-gap` from the app sidebar (`--pbk-pull` = content gap − rail gap; text stays on the content edge), the list's left edge on that same line with its items padded back onto the content edge, the list 8px under the divider and the settings 20px (the preset name, 28.8px in the text colour, heads them), 20px between the list and the settings and 20px right of the settings. At ≥1024px the rows (`.pbk-phead`, `.pbk-pgrid`) follow every tab's content width rule — capped at `--content-max-width`, left-aligned, their padding outside the cap — while the pane itself spans the page. Under 761px it is one ordinary column. (Sticky against the app's own scroller — per-frame `position: fixed`, `contain: size` columns, IntersectionObserver pinned looks, end-of-page measurements — was tried and removed: the list kept being dragged at the page's end.); DocVex and the unsaved "Custom rules" item don't show it. The documents are still the account's, not the preset's. The rules are edited as settings ALONE (one column; a press on a setting's section — not on a choice — CYCLES it to its next choice; switching preset plays the Activity tab's switch motion on the detail AND on the drawer's document — keyed remount / `enter` on WordDocView, from the side the selection moved toward in the list) — the page's former view switch (What it looks like / In Word) and the example column beside the settings ("What that looks like" + Conventions) were removed at the user's request; the example is the Word drawer, which is OPEN on arriving at the Playbook (`wordOpen`, the PAGE's state, starts true; a **Preview** switch at the masthead's far right (`components/Toggle` — the shared on/off switch, `.tgl` in Toggle.css: one pill on and off, the accent only on the knob and a tint on its track, the track faded while off, frosted, the sidebar items' spotlight; stood in the Design system gallery as **Toggle**), above its divider — PageMasthead's `actions` — opens and closes it; the Open in Word button was removed at the user's request) and slides `WordDrawer` in from the RIGHT of the screen — the In Word DOCUMENT alone (`WordRulesView bare`, no choices column) in a fixed, portalled panel floating over the page, the sidebar's surface, RESIZED by a handle on its left edge (`.pbk-drawer-grip`: drag, ←/→, double-click = 836px; clamped 420–1400px and to the window; kept per device in `docvex:playbook:word-drawer-w`), the sheets SCALED to fill its width (zoom 0.4–1.8 of a page); closed by ×, Escape or a press outside the Playbook — never by one on the page beside it (`.pbk-rules-split`: presets, settings), so presets can be switched and rules changed while watching the document; the document is INSTANT — `buildWordDoc` remembers the finished, paginated document per rules (its HTML + per rule the indices of the paragraphs it changes; in memory and in localStorage `docvex:playbook:word-doc:v1`, the last 8), so a set of rules seen before is one innerHTML; in-flight builds are shared and `warmWordDocs` builds every preset's document in idle time; a document still being built shows an "Updating…" pill over the one on show; HOVERING a setting on the page (`.pbk-rule` → `hoverKey`) lights up in it every paragraph that setting changes — an accent wash with an accent edge, every other paragraph faded to 0.32 (`.has-lit`) — and brings the first into view. The slide and the document are kept APART: the panel slides in empty and the document is only built once it has arrived (building it is heavy main-thread work that made the slide snap); after the first opening the panel stays mounted, hidden when closed, so reopening is instant) or **In Word** (`WordRulesView`): a WHOLE sample contract written to the rules in its plain formatting (Times New Roman — the house style `sampleDocx(…, { theme: 'docvex' })` can write was tried here and turned off at the user's request) — `sampleWord(rules)` in lib/rulesSample, the Services contract's .docx plus its blocks, `sampleKeyed` marking each rule on the FIRST block that shows it (flags from `sampleBlocks`: `date` / `amount` / `term` / `blank`, item `role` sub / deep / list, `deepOff` when the deeper level is switched off; a rule nothing shows goes on the first block) — drawn by docx-preview INSIDE the tab as Word draws it: A4 sheets. docx-preview does not paginate, so `paginateSheets` does: the new render is laid out in a hidden stage beside the sheets on show, poured onto sheets of the page's own height, then swapped in — nothing blinks. Each marked block's paragraph is found by its text in document order; its rule choices (SMALL, on ONE line — never wrapping — each rule a hairline SECTION of its own, `.pbk-wrule`, under its name as a small label; HOVERING a rule lights up every paragraph it changes, `.pbk-whl`, from `affects` on the blocks — `AFFECTS` in lib/rulesSample — and re-lights after a pick rebuilds the document) stand in a GUTTER to the RIGHT of the sheets (`.pbk-word-gutter`, 232px), level with that paragraph and pushed down only to clear the one above (`placeStrips`, re-run on resize) — outside the document, so they never change where its pages break. The gutter is NOT held to the content width cap: it is placed absolutely past the sheets' right edge and may run into the page margin up to the pane's right edge; the sheets keep a page's width (`--pbk-word-sheets-w`) and are CSS-zoomed (`--pbk-word-zoom`) only when the pane has no room for page + gutter. Each pick rebuilds the document. "Anything else" is the gutter's LAST section (`.pbk-wextra`), placed under the last rule by `placeStrips` — a React child of the gutter, not rebuilt with the document, so typing keeps focus. (The former **Sample document** dropdown, which opens `SampleDocViewer`: the rules written out as a WHOLE contract by `lib/rulesSample.js` (`sampleBlocks(rules, kind)` → blocks with every label and convention settled — section headings, clause numbering / restart, clause names, sub-points, deep points, list bullets, blanks, dates, amounts, parties (short form incl. the Romanian dative / bare forms), defined terms in their grammatical case, language, the extra rules as a closing note; `sampleDocx` → a real A4 .docx, Times New Roman 12, justified, a borderless signature table) rendered by docx-preview in a modal over the page, with the dropdown to switch sample and Download .docx — `SampleDocViewer` is no longer mounted.) Verified in Node: both samples in both languages under two rule sets, and the .docx is a valid zip. Styled on the design-system tokens (no card fill, hairlines, the gallery's button recipe, the soft status pill, control-token options). **Stored in `localStorage` (`docvex.docRules.v1`), so unlike the writing profile they do NOT follow the account between machines** — that needs a `rules jsonb` column on `writing_profiles`; everything outside `loadDocRules`/`saveDocRules` is written against the returned object, so moving the store is those two functions. |
| **Identity records are GONE — only Data collections** | At the user's request the identity file type (`.dvx`, JSON records in an Identities/ folder), the record form (`IdentityPane`) and its successor section (`CollectionRecord`), the side panel's record Sources tab, the Files tab's Add person / company and Create data collection (`createIdentitiesFromFiles`), the Doc Viewer paragraph filler's "save as identity" (`onCreateIdentity` no longer passed), the Timeline's cast-list step (`extractIdentities` / `saveExtractedIdentities`), the Advisor writing records (it now refuses `.dvc` / `.dvx` and points to the scan), the `.dvx` converter and every record writer (`writeIdentity`, `saveIdentityAt`, `identityBlob`, `mergeIdentity`…) were REMOVED. Data collections are made ONLY by the Files tab's AI scan and are read-only. What the AI reads about a person or a company is still kept INSIDE its collection as `record` (filled by the scan's `fillRecord`, empty fields only), because **document autofill** reads it: `listProjectIdentities` → `readIdentityIfRecord` → `looksLikeIdentityJson` (a `.dvc` whose `record` is a person / org) → `parseIdentity`. `lib/identities` keeps the record as DATA only — its fields, normalising, clause helpers. The Doc Viewer shows a collection as ONE page (`DataCollectionView`): the record's values appear in **What the AI gathered** beside the scan's facts, merged ONE ROW PER VALUE (`mergeFacts`: values compared by their words — equal, or every word of a ≥4-letter value inside a longer one; the AI's label, the fuller value and every source kept), then face matches, timeline, sources, connections and the web. Verified in Node: a scan's collection carries the record and autofill lists the party; the merge on a real CI + contract collection leaves one row per value. |
| `dataCollections.js` / `faceMatch.js` | **The Files tab's AI scan and Data collections.** The sparkle button right of the Files search (`onScanFiles` / `scanState`, `fxScanFiles` in ProjectFiles; pressed again = stop) reads the files TAGGED for the scan (`lib/scanTags.js`: right-click a file or folder → **Tag for AI scan** / Remove from AI scan, bulk-aware; kept per project folder in localStorage `docvex:scan-tags:v1:<dir>` as paths inside the project — a folder as `dir/` covering everything under it, an untagged file inside a tagged folder as an exclusion `!rel`, the nearest mark wins; a tagged item wears the AI mark `.fx-scan-mark` on its thumbnail; nothing tagged → the button says to tag first; untagging a file drops it from the collections on the next scan): a picture by Extract text (`extractImageText`), audio by Generate captions, a video by captions ONLY (its audio track — the pictures are never looked at), anything else by `readSourceText`, an identity record as its JSON. A recording is refused BEFORE it is loaded when it is over 300 MB (video) / 25 MB (audio) — transcription loads the whole file into the app, and a 1 GB video stalled the window — and once one transcription fails for want of the service (no OpenAI key, offline) the rest of the recordings are skipped. Files are understood a batch at a time AS THEY ARE READ, each understanding saved at once, so a stopped or crashed scan resumes where it was. The AI then understands each file (batches of ≤8 / ~40k chars, sonnet, `usageAction: 'files-scan'`) — summary, subject, facts, named entities, dates, and `idDocument` when the file IS an identity document — saved per file as the AI data facet **`understanding`** (shown in the Doc Viewer's Data tab, pictures included). Then the files are CONNECTED into **Data collections**, one per real-world subject: `.dvc` files (JSON, `type: 'docvex/data-collection'`: title, subject, summary, `sources` [{ name, rel, kind, method, role, understood }], facts / timeline with the files they come from, connections, `entities`, `related`, `faceMatches` / `faceReference`), written into the project root, wearing a web glyph (`extCategory` → `collection`), grouped under **Data collections** by Group by category, opened in the Doc Viewer as `components/DataCollectionView` (kind `collection`: summary, facts, face matches with both faces cropped and the score, timeline, sources with thumbnails and what was understood, how the files connect, THE WEB — linked collections and the names held; every file a button that opens it). **Incremental — the WEB INDEX** `.docvex-web.json` (a dotfile in the project root, never listed; mirrored in localStorage `docvex:data-web:v1:<dir>`): every scanned file's size + mtime + understanding, and the collections written. A second run: nothing new → NO AI call and no write; files removed → handled locally (their facts, timeline entries, connections and source rows dropped; a collection left with no source goes to the Trash); files added or changed → only those are read and understood, and ONE small call (`integrateFiles`) shows the AI just them plus a card per collection (title + subject; summary and names only for collections sharing a name with the new files) and merges its `updates` / `created` answer locally. A file that yields nothing is remembered as skipped until it changes. Links between collections (shared files, shared names folded by `foldName` — diacritics, case, legal forms dropped) and each collection's entities are computed locally every run. Verified in Node with the file system and AI stubbed: first run 2 calls, unchanged 0 calls / 0 writes, one file added 2 small calls, a file removed 0 calls. **FACE MATCHING** (`lib/faceMatch.js`): an identity document's photo (a picture the AI flagged `idDocument`, or whose reading names a CI / passport / permis) is compared with every face in the project's pictures by **@vladmandic/face-api** running LOCALLY (TensorFlow.js; SSD MobileNet v1 + 68 landmarks + the 128-number recogniser; models copied to `public/faces/` by `scripts/copy-face-assets.mjs`, postinstall, gitignored; loaded by XHR + `decodeWeights` / `loadFromWeightMap` so packaged file:// works; lazily imported). Faces are biometric data: descriptors are kept only in this machine's AI data (facet `faces`, `local: true` — account sync leaves it out) and never go to an AI service; only the result is written into the collection. Confidence = logistic of the descriptor distance centred at 0.5 (0.40 → 86%, 0.50 → 50%; shown from 50%, labelled Strong ≥85% / Likely ≥65% / Possible); a document photographed sideways is tried at the other quarter-turns; two identity documents with the same face link too. Matched pictures join the holder's collection as `method: 'face'` sources (re-derived every run, so a match that no longer holds goes); with no collection for the holder one is made locally. NOT verified in the app: model loading and detection (Node can't run the browser tfjs build), the scan end to end, the viewer pane. |
| Phone upload — `components/PhoneUploadModal`, `src/phoneUploadServer.js`, `lib/phoneUploadCloud.js`, `lib/phoneUploadPage.js`, `supabase/functions/phone-upload`, migration 040 | **The Files tab's Import opens a window** (ProjectFiles `importOpen`): "Choose files from this computer" (the old picker) or a PHONE by QR code, into the folder on show — ONE code at a time: the local network's, and a Local network / DocVex cloud switch above the code — the Playbook's segmented control, `components/RuleOptions` (lifted out of Playbook.jsx with its CSS, `RuleOptions.css`; classes still `pbk-rule-opts` / `-pill` / `-opt` so the Playbook's own overrides reach them) — that shows the cloud's instead. The card is the code on the left and, beside it, the route's name (its tag in the section's top-right corner), the address note and the points — no frame, no link text. **LIVE PHOTOS — the partner rule** (phoneUploadServer `holdArrived` / `holdPartner` / `holdLivp`, both routes — the cloud one goes through `hold-file`): a video named `<picture stem>.live.<mov|mp4|m4v|webm>` (Wi-Fi: with `partner=<the picture's held id>`) is never a waiting file of its own — no tile is started for it (`quiet`), it is kept as `.docvex-incoming/<picture id>.live.<ext>` on the picture's entry (`live`, re-attached after a restart), announced as a `live` event (the Import window's tile shows a **LIVE** mark, `.pum-live`), and follows the picture's decision: accepted → written as `<the picture's final stem>.<ext>` (so a picture that became "Photo 1 (2).jpg" gets "Photo 1 (2).mp4"); rejected / withdrawn → deleted. A movement for a picture already ACCEPTED waits on its own under the final stem (`acceptedStems`, `decided[].final`); one for a REJECTED picture is refused (409 `rejected`, the page says so); one that arrives BEFORE its picture (the cloud route can deliver out of order) waits `ORPHAN_MS` (90s) and is attached when the picture is held, else listed on its own. A `.livp` upload is unpacked into a waiting picture named after it with its video attached (an unreadable one waits as it is). Verified in Node against the server: picture + movement by id, accept with a name clash (both renamed alike), a late movement, a `.livp`, reject clearing the movement, a movement refused after reject, and on the cloud route movement-before-picture and picture-before-movement. NOT verified on a phone: the camera recording (iOS `MediaRecorder` of a canvas stream). **The "Send to DocVex" Shortcut** (iPhone, Wi-Fi route): an Apple Shortcut can take a Live Photo's movement where a page cannot, and posts straight to the Wi-Fi server — the still (`/file?name=<Name>.jpg`, its JSON `id` read back) then the movement (`/file?name=<Name>.live.mov&partner=<id>`, from **Encode Media**), the portal URL taken by **Scan QR or Barcode**. iPhone installs only Shortcuts shared from an iPhone (Apple signs them), so it cannot be generated here: the page shows the build steps in a fold (`.howto`, iOS + Wi-Fi only), and once one is built and shared with "Copy iCloud Link", pasting that link into `SHORTCUT_URL` (lib/phoneUploadPage) makes the fold offer it as one tap. Not yet built or tested on a phone — whether Encode Media yields the movement of a Live Photo is unconfirmed. **Same Wi-Fi — Local network** (desktop only): main runs an HTTP server on the LAN (`0.0.0.0`, ports 47810–47815 else any; the QR is `http://<lan ip>:<port>/u/<token>`, the likeliest private address first, virtual adapters last); a random 144-bit token compared in constant time and KEPT per project (`lib/phoneUploadLocal`, localStorage `docvex:phone-upload:local:v2:<userId>:<projectId>` → `{ token, dir, folder, project, port }`): the address is the SAME every time the window opens, it goes on receiving with the window CLOSED — and EVERY file, window open or not and by either route, is HELD for approval (nothing reaches a project unapproved; a cloud download goes into the same waiting list through `phone-upload:hold-file`; with the window open its Received section shows the waiting files as plain tiles (no faded / download look): each is TICKED (a click unticks / reticks it — ticked = the Files tab's selected tile + a filled check, `.pum-tick`; unticked = faded; the tiles have no × any more), and the **Import** button in the window's bottom-right corner (`.pum-foot`, sticky) imports the ticked ones, DISCARDS the unticked (a note beside it says how many) and then CLOSES the window; CLOSING the window (×, Escape, a click outside) with files still waiting asks first (`ConfirmModal` — "will be lost") and, on Discard, rejects them all; the PHONE PAGE then shows them as **Rejected** with **Send again** / **Remove** — it asks `GET /u/<token>/status?ids=` every 3s about the files it sent (the server keeps each decision, `decided`, newest 2000, answering only for that link's own files; Wi-Fi route only — the cloud page cannot hear back without a status column on `phone_upload_files` and an Edge Function change) — and the toast is held back, `setIncomingQuiet`): the server writes the file to `<folder>/.docvex-incoming/<id>__<name>` (never listed) the file then STANDS IN THE FILES TAB, in the folder it was sent to, FADED with a download mark (ProjectFiles `incomingItems` from `lib/phoneUploadIncoming`'s store; FilesWorkspace `item.incoming`, `.is-incoming` / `.fx-incoming-mark`): a CLICK accepts it; its right-click menu is Accept — add to this folder / Reject (confirmed in a modal, like Delete) / Properties — one file at a time, no preview; it can't be dragged, renamed, cut or copied, and Delete means Reject. Its arrival is announced by `components/PhoneIncomingNotifier` (headless, in AppShell) through the app's own notifications (`notify()`, the bottom-right toast stack): "File arrived from your phone", name · size · the folder it waits in, its thumbnail in the icon's place (NotificationToast renders `payload.thumb` / `payload.thumbExt` as `.toast-thumb` — any file notification can carry one), and **Show in Files** (the action registry's `file` builder) — **Accept** (moved into the folder under a free name) / **Reject** (deleted), Accept all / Reject all over several; held files outlive a restart (a session start rereads the folder; IPC `phone-upload:hold / pending / accept / reject`) and the phone page marks it "Waiting for approval" (its take-back × was removed at the user's request; the server's `POST /u/<token>/withdraw?id=` route stays, unused — only a file that session sent and only while it waits, else 409 `not_waiting`; the held copy is deleted and a `withdrawn` event drops it from the Files tab); **SEVERAL PEOPLE, ONE NETWORK / ONE COMPUTER:** a file only reaches the app whose token its link carries (every computer has its own server and random tokens — a phone at an address that moved to another machine gets a 404); on one computer each session has an OWNER (the signed-in account): kept links are stored per account, `setIncomingOwner` (PhoneIncomingNotifier, on every account change) releases every other owner's session and lists only that account's waiting files, so the next person signed in never gets the last one's; the PORT is kept with the link and tried first (`listen(prefer)`), so a saved phone address reaches this instance again rather than another DocVex on the machine; and the phone page names the computer (`os.hostname()`). **THE CLOUD ROUTE BEHAVES AS THE WI-FI ONE** (migration 041 + the function's `create` reopen / `status`): its address is kept per account and project (`docvex:phone-upload:cloud:v1:<user>:<project>` → `{ token, sessionId }`, "New address" makes a fresh one), closing the window PAUSES it (`close` → closed_at; the page shows "Upload closed" and comes back on reopen), files wait for approval and the phone learns the outcome. **THE PORTAL LIVES ONLY WHILE THE IMPORT WINDOW IS OPEN** (at the user's request): closing the window stops the Wi-Fi session (`phoneUploadStop`; the server closes once nothing uses it) and kept addresses are NOT revived at startup any more (`resumeLocalLinks` is no longer called); the ADDRESS is still kept, so reopening the window brings the same page back. The phone page checks every 3s (`/info`, 2.5s timeout; the cloud page every 6s through the function) and, when the portal is gone, swaps its buttons for an "Upload closed" notice (list kept) — coming back by itself when the window reopens (Wi-Fi; the cloud page asks for the new code). Verified in Electron against the server: open → closed notice within ~6s of the stop → back after a restart on the same address, so a phone can keep its page open; files go to the folder the window was last opened on (the phone page re-reads it per batch); it lapses after 7 days unused (sliding — each open and each file pushes it back) and "New address" revokes it; everything else 404; a file streams to a hidden `.docvex-upload-<id>.part` beside its destination and is renamed into place when complete (never over an existing file — `scan (2).jpg`), ≤4 GB; nothing leaves the network. Windows asks once to let DocVex through the firewall; an isolated guest network blocks it. **Any connection — DocVex cloud** (made the moment the switch is turned on; the session lasts until the window closes, so switching back and forth keeps both): `startCloudUpload` → the `phone-upload` Edge Function (verify_jwt OFF; `create` / `close` check the JWT themselves) mints a session (`phone_upload_sessions`, only the token's SHA-256 kept, 30 min) and the QR is `https://docvex.ro/upload.html?t=<token>`; the phone asks `info`, then per file `sign` (a signed upload URL into the private `phone-upload` bucket, `<session>/<uuid>`, 50 MB) → PUT → `done` (the object is checked, a `phone_upload_files` row written); the desktop hears it (Realtime + a 5 s poll), downloads it into the folder under a free name and DELETES the object and the row — the bucket is a hand-off. Sessions a day past their end are swept (objects + rows) on the next `create`. **One page for both routes**, styled by the DESIGN SYSTEM on the app's SPOTLIGHT BACKGROUND (the backdrop, the dot grid of AppShell's `.main-content::before` and CursorSpotlight's brighter dots — NOT following anything: always visible at a resting glow, they glide slowly to a random spot, flare up there and settle back; the Take a photo button is frosted over them) (token values copied in — Cream, Ink when the phone is dark; Inter + Plus Jakarta Sans; the masthead's eyebrow / display title / hairline; rows on the page ground with hairlines; soft pills; the gallery's buttons at a thumb's height) with a THUMBNAIL per file (the picture read on the phone, else the FILES TAB'S OWN TYPE ICON — `components/fileGlyph` ExtGlyph rendered to static markup by `scripts/build-phone-glyphs.mjs` into `lib/phoneUploadGlyphs.js` (icons, extension → category, fileGlyph.css; re-run it when an icon changes — build-upload-page runs it too) and HANDED to the page as `cfg.glyphs` by the server and the cloud build, since the dev hot reload evaluates the page file alone and cannot follow an import; drawn in Ink's icon colours as in the app; the desktop Import window's arrival list shows the saved file's thumbnail too): `phoneUploadPage({ mode })` — served by the desktop server in `local` mode, and written to `landing/home/upload.html` in `cloud` mode by `npm run site:upload-page` (then `site:deploy`); **HOT RELOAD under `npm start`**: the local server does not serve the copy bundled into main — it re-reads `src/lib/phoneUploadPage.js` on every request (its `export`s stripped, evaluated with `new Function`; a file that fails to evaluate keeps the last good version and logs why), and the page (`dev: true`) polls `/u/<token>/dev` (the file's mtime) every second and reloads on a change, never mid-upload — so a phone with the page open shows each save; packaged builds use the bundled copy; phone-first, Choose files / **Take photos** (on a secure page — the cloud route — an in-page camera, `getUserMedia`, that stays open: one shutter press per photo, each uploading as the next is taken, Done closes it; on the plain-http Wi-Fi page the browser allows only the phone's own camera, one photo a press, and the button then reads "Take another photo"), per-file progress, two uploads at a time. The window shows every arrival UNDER the QR code section as the Files tab's own tiles (`FileTile`, exported from FilesWorkspace — the `.fx-grid` / `.fx-tile` markup without menus or selection), newest first, each with a reject × on hover (into the project's Trash, via ProjectFiles' `primTrash` — the `onReject` prop): faded with a progress bar along the thumbnail while it comes, the saved file's thumbnail once it is on disk (double-click opens it); route and size in its tooltip. Verified in Node: the local server end to end (page, info, two uploads of one name → `scan.jpg` + `scan (2).jpg`, a `../` name flattened, a wrong token 404, closed on stop). LIVE since 2026-09-26: migration 040 applied and `phone-upload` deployed (verify_jwt off); verified against production with a test session — info, sign, the raw PUT to the signed URL, done (row + counts written), a 99 MB file refused, a bad token 404, `create` without a JWT 401 — then cleaned up. NOT verified: the desktop's download/delete leg, the page on docvex.ro (needs `npm run site:deploy` + push) and a real phone. |
| `sourceChecks.js` + `components/SourcesCheckCard` | **Every AI-written document is checked against the connected sources.** ONE REGISTRY, one entry per platform (`SOURCES`: `id`, `label`, `tab` = its `LEGAL_TABS` id, `available()`, `find(text)` → refs, `check(ref)` → `{ status: ok/warn/error/unknown, title, detail, facts, excerpts, source }`, or — not connected yet — `plan`). Connected today: **legislatie.just.ro** (acts by number/year AND codes by name — mapped to their laws, `CODES` — merged per act; exists? repealed? the title the document gives vs the real one; every article cited QUOTED from the act's text, a missing or repealed article flagged; EU acts are left to EUR-Lex), **anaf.ro** (each CUI: exists, inactive / struck off, named as ANAF names it, its trade register number), **insse.ro** (CAEN: exists, Rev. 2 codes no longer in force → their Rev. 3 class, numbers whose meaning changed), **portal.just.ro** (court files). Waiting (`plan`, refs already FOUND and listed as waiting): eur-lex, bpi, termene/listafirme, ancpi, rejust, unbr. **Connecting a platform = give its entry a `check`** (and drop `stub` in LEGAL_TABS) — the Doc Viewer, the brief and the report pick it up with no other change. `checkText(text)` runs every connected entry (≤14 refs each, 3 at a time, memoised 15 min — a corrected draft citing the same acts costs nothing); an answer from the machine's COPY that doesn't know an item is `unknown`, never "doesn't exist". **In the Doc Viewer** (`checkAgainstSources`, called by `applyGenResult` after every version the AI writes): a `role: 'sources'` message (the card, under the version: one row per item, verdict, source, folded facts and the official article text, then the waiting platforms) and — when a source contradicts the draft — ONE automatic correction turn (`correctionPrompt`: the problems with the sources' own words + what was confirmed; `runTurn(…, { correction: true })`, which is checked but never corrected again, so nothing loops). **In the brief** (DocBrief's Generate): the acts picked as the legal basis and the parties' CUIs are checked BEFORE drafting and `groundingBlock` rides in the request (capped at 25s). Verified in Node with the portal / ANAF stubbed: act + code merged, art. quoted, missing article and invented law flagged, CAEN Rev. 2 flagged, offline court file `unknown`, second run 0 calls. |
| `docBrief.js` + `components/DocBrief` | **The brief — between picking a template and the draft.** On a blank document's "What do you want to make?", picking a template (or "Something else") no longer starts the draft: it opens a STAGED questionnaire built on the anatomy every Romanian legal document shares, one step at a time (`BRIEF_STEPS`): 1 Tipul și scopul (Ce?) · 2 Părțile (Cine cu cine?) · 3 Temeiul juridic și contextul (De ce?) · 4 Regulile jocului și sancțiunile · 5 Validarea și jurisdicția (Unde și cum?). The structure is UNIVERSAL — every document gets every step's questions; a template only ADDS its own (`BRIEF_QUESTIONS` with `when`: an NDA asks what is secret and for how long, a sale the goods, a pleading the court, claims, value, evidence and costs). Questions are pills / typed / "Other…", and every one but parties, collections and preset can be handed to the AI ("Let the AI decide", or "the rest" in the foot bar). **Parties:** role (guessed from the label, `defaultRoles`), persoană fizică or juridică, then the details from a Data collection holding a record, TYPED (PJ: denumire, formă, CUI, ORC, sediu, IBAN, bancă, reprezentant; PF: nume, prenume, CNP, serie/număr CI, domiciliu — `PARTY_FIELDS`, keyed by the record keys the blanks use) with **Fill from ANAF** on the CUI (`lookupCompanies`, desktop only, `fieldsFromAnaf`), or left as blanks. **Setup:** the Data collections to draw on (their summary, facts and timeline go into the prompt) and the Playbook preset (one other than the preset in use rides in the request via `buildDocRulesSteer`, which the rules block says outranks it). The foot bar (Back · progress · N questions left · Next · **Generate**) — Generate takes the reader to the first open question until every one is answered, then `buildBriefPrompt` (template outline + answers per step + parties + collections + preset) goes to the advisor as the first turn. Verified in Node: an NDA brief's question set, party roles, completeness and the prompt. |
| `briefFromNetwork.js` | **The chooser and the brief READ FROM THE NEURAL NETWORK (2026-09-28).** What the live network / AI scan understood of the project's files (lib/caseInsights `loadCaseData`: every file's passport + the Data collections, as a compact digest `networkDigest`, ≤60k chars) sets the document up instead of a blank questionnaire. `suggestFromNetwork` — "What do you want to make?" shows **From your files** above the search (`NetworkSuggestions` in DocViewer.jsx): one line on what the case is and where it stands, and 3–6 cards for the documents it most likely needs next (catalogue templates, or a titled document the catalogue lacks), each with the fact that calls for it and the files that say so; picking one opens the brief with that reason as its `hint`. `prefillBrief` — the brief (components/DocBrief) is ANSWERED FROM THE FILES once its collections are in: parties (a Data collection's record when one is that party — named by the AI or matched by name — else the details as the files write them, else blanks), the collections to draw on, and every question the files answer (option ids validated, free text only where allowed); each marked **From your files** (`AutoMark`, the files in its tooltip), what the files don't say listed under the title (`NetworkNote`). A question the user touched is never overwritten (`touched`); editing an answer drops its mark. One sonnet call each (`brief-suggest` / `brief-prefill`, through lib/projectAi so pseudonymisation applies), cached per device against a signature of the network (`docvex:brief-network:suggest:v1:<dir>` / `…prefill:v1:<dir>:<template>:<hash>`) — a repeat costs nothing, a file the live network adds asks again. No scanned files → a line saying to tag files, no call. Verified in Node with the AI stubbed (suggestion validation, answer / party / collection mapping, the cache); NOT verified in the app. |
| `metadataPrefetch.js` | Background sweep that extracts + caches file metadata (`metadataHistory.js`) as files appear in a folder, so the Doc Viewer's Metadata tab is pre-filled. One file at a time, on idle, size-capped, cancelled by the next listing. |
| `activityMetrics.js` | Personal Activity-page metrics (heatmap, streak, breakdown). |
| `aiEngine.js` | **THE ONE AI** (2026-09-28) — Research and the Doc Viewer's advisor both run on it; change it here and both change. What each surface may do is DATA (`AI_SURFACES`: research = portals, Auto, legislation highlight, project files; viewer = all of that + the open file + CREATE files (write_document / ask_user) + EDIT files (paragraphs in place, `docvex-edit` blocks with Undo)). NO standing rules: a turn is the last `historyTurns` messages + the question with its DATA in front, in self-describing blocks (`<open_file>`, `<historical_legal_context>`, `<portal_record source ref>`, `<project_files>`); the only instructions are the ones a capability needs (`AI_PROMPTS`: router, research summary, drafting, current / foreign document, `paragraphFrame`; plus lib/aiFileEdits `EDIT_RULE` and, on a draft, the Playbook rules + writing style). `prepareTurn` gathers in PARALLEL: Auto (`chooseModel`, Haiku router with the user's prompt, 8s then Sonnet 5), the portal records the question names (`gatherPortals`: `findFollowableRefs` → `viewForRef` → lib/portalRecords `recordText`, ≤4, 15s), the project digest (`projectContext`, cached 5 min, 12s, only with the switch on). `askAi` enforces `AI_LIMITS` (2 min an answer, 4 min a draft). ONE setting for both surfaces (`docvex:ai:model:v1`, `docvex:ai:project-files:v1`; `components/AiControls` — the model picker + Project files switch, `useAiSettings`, synced across windows).
