The app sidebar writes an ANAF tab's legal form short (`shortLegalForm`: SOCIETATE CU RĂSPUNDERE LIMITATĂ → SRL, SA, SRL-D, SNC, SCS, SCA, PFA, II, IF…). **A PAGE WRITING ITS OWN ADDRESS IS NOT AN ARRIVAL**: CAEN puts the code it shows into `?code=` (opening one, restoring the one it had), and the tabs read every such change as a link from elsewhere — a new pending page in the tab, the spinner, the code re-opened, on every entry into a CAEN tab; its writes now carry `state: { internal: true }` and LegalWorkspace's arrival effect skips them. A CAEN tab's kind is its REVISION ("insse.ro · Rev. 3"), not "Class" (Caen.jsx items, lib/legalSearch rows; pageMeta maps tabs stored with "Class"). **A TAB'S LABEL IS NEVER TOUCHED** (`named` in lib/legalBrowser): it stays what the tab was opened with — what was typed ("CUI 2408422"), a link's label, a search result's name — the platform page only filling in what is missing (no kind, no title, or "Opening…"). What the page LOADS for the item (its own name — the company's, "LEGE · nr. 31/1990") is kept beside it ONCE AND FOR GOOD (`loaded` on the page, stored with the tabs, so remembered across restarts) and shown as the tab's custom Tooltip (`loadedTip` from pageMeta) in the app sidebar and in the Legislation tab's rail. Tabs saved by the short-lived `asked` / `resolved` version are converted on load (their search back as the label, the loaded name into `loaded`). **ONE SEARCH TAB** (`isSearchTab` / `searchTabOf` / `openSearch` in lib/legalBrowser): searching never piles up tabs — there is at most one BLANK tab (still on the new-tab page it opened on), it is not listed as a tab, and the rail's former + is a **Search** tab button standing first in the list (`SearchTabButton`, `.lgb-searchtab`, a magnifier where a tab has its dot; selected while the blank tab is on show) that goes to it, making it only when there is none; a search run from it turns it into an ordinary tab. Ctrl+T, the tab menu's Search, the strip's first tab and the app sidebar's Legislation row all go through `openSearch`; the sidebar's list leaves the blank tab out too. **The rail is not drawn while the only tab is the search tab** (one tab, a new-tab page). **It fills the space above it** (`useRailFill` in LegalWorkspace): where the header's second line holds nothing over the rail's width (a new tab; an item whose only control is the find at the right), the rail is lifted into that band by a transform and grown by as much, its foot unchanged, standing above the bar there (`.is-lifted`, z 31); a layout change eases (`.is-easing`, 300ms), a scroll follows at once. Its STICKY geometry comes from constants, never from the bar's pinned class (which LegalTabs flips a few pixels early and a frame late): its sticky top (`--lgb-rail-top`, written inline) is always the bar's sticky top + the bar's height + the head gap, both against the same scrollport origin (the scroller's top + border + padding), so the two stick at the same scroll; its place is max(the shell's top, that sticky top); its height runs from there to one inset above the window's bottom; and the shell's min-height is written to exactly the stuck rail plus its bottom margin, so sticking never runs out of room. The pin / unpin flip updates at once, never eased. It never overlaps what stands above it: a PINNED bar is taken whole (its frosted ground covers its box), while a bar in flow is transparent and its rows AND ITS WHOLE SECOND LINE count (the line under the divider is the header's band wherever its controls stand — a find at its far right used to leave the left "empty" and the rail rose over the header; only a line2 with no box of its own falls back to its controls over the rail's width) — the rail rises only into what is empty and shrinks back when something is there. The new tab page no longer lists "Recently closed" (Ctrl+Shift+T and the tab menu still reopen). **The tabs rail is the Playbook presets list** (`.lgb-rail` = `.pbk-prail` — keep them in step; its width the list width plus the divider pull, as the Playbook's): items are `.lg-rail-item` (two lines, × on hover, the tab on show sticky inside the list), a head with the label, History and + over a hairline, a divider after the pinned tabs, the sidebar surface (frosted page ground / Cream `--bg-sidebar` 90%, 1.5px ring, 9.6px corners, the card shadow) and the pointer spotlight (`useRailSpotlight` + `useItemSpots`); sticky under the bar, its FOOT always one inset above the window's bottom — before it sticks its height grows with the scroll (scroll-driven `lgb-rail-grow` over `--lgb-travel`, the distance the page scrolls before the bar sticks, measured in LegalWorkspace), and the shell is kept at least the stuck rail's height. The rest of LegalBrowser.css is on the Design system's recipes (control field + tint via `color-mix`, ONE soft pill recipe, the tab bar's soft toggle for the scopes, List rows, the dropdown recipe for the tab menu, label tokens). The same pass fixed the platform pages: LegalSourceStub's level pills (soft, sentence case) and access note (no fill), Caen's outline frame / search field / atlas entry (bar surface) / tile selection / pill kind, CaenModal's head band and chips, `#fff` → `--text-on-accent` in LegalWorkspace / CaenPicker, card shadows → `--ds-card-shadow`, and wrong `--ds-card-radius` fallbacks (10/12px → 14px). NOT verified in the app (bundles only): the layout, pinning, pending binding, the sidebar list. The text from here on is the PREVIOUS design, kept for its details of the platform pages. — **ONE SEARCH ENGINE (2026-09-27, at the user's request):** the platforms are no longer a row of tabs and no page has its own search form any more. `LegalWorkspace` draws ONE header for every platform ("Legislation", `source: <site>` as the muted eyebrow, and an **i** button — `PlatformsButton` in `components/LegalOmnibox` — opening a popover of every platform in `LEGAL_TABS` with what it is (`about`), Connected / Coming, and example searches (`tries`) — informational only, nothing in it is clickable), then the bar's line (`LegalTabs standalone dropSearch`): **the one search** (`LegalOmnibox` — the dice joined to a full-width field; Ctrl/⌘+L focuses it, Ctrl/⌘+F stays the open act's find; its text is module-level so it survives moving between the platforms' pages), the page's `tools`, the open act's find, the status pill (it stands on this line now — `standalone` renders `status` there) and the page's `trailing` + History. On Enter, `detectQuery` in **`lib/legalOmni.js`** reads what was typed and navigates to the platform with it in the route (plus a nonce `_`, so the same search runs twice): a platform prefix wins (`anaf …`/`cui …`, `caen …`, `dosar …`/`dosare …`, `parte …`); else by shape — a court file number (3–4 parts, year third) → `/portal-just?nr=`; RO+digits or 5–10 digits with a valid CUI check digit (a list too) → `/anaf?cui=`; 2–4 digits (not a year) → `/caen?code=&open=1`; an act (`Legea 31/1990`, `OUG nr. 195/2002`, `HG 1383 2022`, `Ordinul 228 din 2012`, or any citation `findLawRefs` knows) → `/legislation?tip&nr&an&open=1`; `31/1990` → by number and year; a bare year → that year's acts; anything else → words (`?titlu=`). A pill at the field's end says where Enter will go. The dice (`randomQuery`) draws something that exists from a random platform (the draws the pages used to make) and writes it into the field without running it. CAEN takes `?q=` (words) and `?code=…&open=1` from it. `WorkspaceSearch` now ignores `modes`/`title`/`sub`: before an answer it is the one start screen, which holds THE SEARCH itself, larger (`LegalOmnibox large`), and everywhere else the same search as a compact row at the top of the main column — drawn by LegalWorkspace over EVERY tab (an answer, an open act / company / court file / CAEN class, a platform not yet connected), the page's `foot` (Clear, its note) portalled beside it through the `SearchFootSlot` context — the search is NOT in the bar's line any more (removed at the user's request; the start screen's registration is a layout effect so the two never paint together); the example chips were removed from it (they remain in the "i" list). **Two tabs, Search and History** (the bar's tabs row again, `LegalTabs ownTabs`; the view is module-level in LegalOmnibox — `useLegalView` / `setLegalView` — so it holds across the platforms' pages, and anything that searches or opens goes back to Search): History replaced the clock button's dropdown — the page's `history` props are drawn INLINE in the main column (`HistoryMenu inline`, `.lg-hist.is-inline`), the platform on show's log, picking an entry returns to Search and runs it; on History the bar drops the act's find, the status pill and the page's `trailing`. **The rail's + makes a SEARCH TAB** (`addSearchTab` & co. in LegalOmnibox, module-level; only ONE blank one at a time — + selects it if it exists): an item "Search · New search" at the top of the open list that shows SEARCH EVERYWHERE (the start screen, `WorkspaceSearch asked={false}`, drawn by LegalWorkspace in place of the page's content — whichever platform's page is under it) until a query is run from it; then it keeps the query as its title and the answering platform's route, and picking it again goes back there; it is titled by the query run from it and gives way to the item opened from it (as a browser tab navigates — LegalWorkspace closes it when the page's `activeId` goes from null to an item while it is active); picking a real item leaves it inactive, × closes it. **Filters are PER TAB** (`useTabSetting(tabKey, name, default)` + `tabKeyFor(route, activeId)` in LegalOmnibox — the open item, else the selected search tab, else the platform's own search view; module-level, in memory): CAEN's View and Revision (no longer remembered per device — `loadRev` / `loadView` are unused, and both pickers are hidden while a class is open) and the in-tab find each belong to the tab they were set in; a tab that never set one reads the default (List, Rev. 3, empty). A new page filter should go through `useTabSetting`. **The line under the divider:** at the LEFT the page's `tools` and its `trailing` (CAEN's View / Revision), then the status pill (`.lws-status`), and at the RIGHT a find — the Files tab's search box (`LegalSearchBox`, Ctrl/⌘+F) searching what the tab shows: every match in the main column (an answer, an open item, the History list) lit with the CSS Custom Highlight API (`useChatFind`, name `legalfind`), count and ‹ › inside the field; a page with a find of its own (an open act's) puts that one there instead. While SEARCH EVERYWHERE (the start screen) is on show the line is EMPTY — no find, no page controls, no status (`useStartScreenSearch` in its `StartSearch`, read by `useSearchOnStart`). **The rail is back in the page** (drawn only while something is open), as the Playbook presets list (`WorkspaceRail card`, `.lg-rail.is-card` — a framed sidebar-surface card from the first frame, a head "Open" with a + that goes back to the search and focuses it, the active item sticky): EVERY platform's open items in one list, like a browser's vertical tabs, each kind line prefixed with the platform (`short`); an item of another platform is opened through `requestWorkspace` + navigation, as the app sidebar's dropdown does (which still lists them too). A page's `masthead` prop is no longer drawn. Verified in Node: `detectQuery` on 26 typed forms; the app bundles. NOT verified in the app: the layout, the popover, the rail across platforms. — **The rail of open items is no longer drawn in the page** — it moved to the APP SIDEBAR: `LegalWorkspace` publishes each tab's `items` / `activeId` under its route to `lib/workspaceItems.js` (module-level, so a tab's list outlives the page) and registers the page's `onSelect` / `onClose` while mounted; the Sidebar's Legislation entry has a fold chevron (`.nav-fold-chev`, `docvex.sidebar.legislationOpen`) and, under it, a dropdown (`.nav-sub`) of every tab's items, each tab headed by a labelled hairline divider (`.nav-sub-div`, the tab's `LEGAL_TABS` label), each item two lines with the Open files row's close button. A click on an item of a tab not on screen QUEUES the select (`requestWorkspace`) and navigates; the tab answers when it mounts, its list back from pageMemory first. A close on an unmounted tab is dropped from the store at once and queued. With items in the list, a click on the Legislation ROW folds it (on a Legislation tab it only collapses / expands; from elsewhere it navigates and opens it), and while the entry is selected and open the selected ground wraps the row AND the list (`.nav-fold.is-wrapped`). Debug → **Simulate items in the Legislation list** (`setWorkspaceSimulation`, `docvex:debug:simulate-workspace`) adds sample `sim:` items after the real ones; selecting / closing one touches only the store. `WorkspaceRail` is still exported for the Design system gallery. The description below is the former in-page rail: the frame every Legislation source tab stands in, **the Newsletter excepted** (legislatie.just.ro, insse.ro/CAEN, portal.just.ro, anaf.ro and the placeholder tabs): masthead, the `LegalTabs` bar whose second line opens with **Search** (the way back to the search and its answers, lit while they are on show; its width is the rail's) and **History** (the clock, `HistoryButton` props via `history`), then the page's own `bar.tools`; below, the RAIL of what the tab has opened (`items: [{ id, kind, title, tip }]`, `activeId`, `onSelect`, `onClose`; not drawn while empty; sticky under the bar, frosted when the bar pins, the sidebar's spotlight, resizable divider — width shared by every tab, `docvex:legislation:rail-w`) and the main column (children). What an item is per tab: an act (reading session), a CAEN class (browsing sections / divisions / groups stays in the tree or picker; a class opens as an item), a court file, an ANAF company (a lookup finding one opens it; a list is shown as rows). `WorkspaceSearchTab` and `WorkspaceRail` (`still` for a static drawing) are exported; the Design system gallery shows the frame live ("Workspace"). A new source tab = render its page inside `LegalWorkspace`. **The search view is shared too**: `WorkspaceSearch({ asked, title, sub, modes: [{ id, label, hint, node }], foot })` — before anything is asked, the empty-state start screen (mark, title, the ways to search stacked with "or", hints, Clear via `WorkspaceClear`); after, the same controls as one compact row. Legislation, Court files (By file / By party, or A court's day) and ANAF (By CUI) draw their search with it in the main column (the tab bar keeps only an open act's find — `noSearch` otherwise); CAEN's list view does (the words box), its picker view has no search. **Where things came from**: `SourceStatus` (the "Live from <site>" (e.g. "Live from anaf.ro") / "From your copy on this machine" pill + "Differs from the portal - click to sync") and `KeptFigures` (the masthead's Kept here / On this machine) are exported for every tab. Court files and ANAF keep what their service answers in **`lib/sourceCache.js`** (per tab, localStorage `docvex:source-cache:<tab>:v1`, ~2.5 MB, oldest out): `searchCasesKept` / `listHearingsKept` / `checkFile` / `keepFile` in lib/courts, `lookupCompaniesKept` / `checkCompany` in lib/anaf — the service first, the copy when it cannot be REACHED (`isUnreachable`), each answer carrying `source: 'live' | 'archive'`; an item opened from the copy is checked in the background and offers Sync when it differs; History's head has "Kept files" / "Kept companies" to forget the copy. CAEN's pill says "Bundled with the app · INS" (nothing is fetched).
- **CourtMap** (`components/CourtMap.jsx` + `.css`, `cmap-`; `lib/courtMap.js`; `lib/roCountiesMap.json` built by `scripts/build-ro-map.mjs` from Natural Earth admin-1, public domain) — portal.just.ro's map of the counties, on Court files' start screen: counties shaded by court of appeal (no two neighbours alike), plates as labels; pressing one glides the map in on its circumscription (`CIRCUMSCRIPTIONS`, the fifteen courts of appeal and their counties) and lists every court there county by county, the pressed county first; pressing a court lists its hearings for today. A court's county is read off its label via the gazetteer (`countyOfCourt`; `TOWN_OVERRIDE` for towns whose name ranks higher in another county — Costești AG, Liești GL); the six military courts have none (`nationalCourts`).
- **SplitView** — single-pane content shell; portals a pane's header/footer chrome into fixed slots via `PaneChromeContext` (it replaced the old multi-pane SplitView + its context).
- **ProjectBanner** — small **fixed-position pill** at top-centre of the viewport ("Working in <project>"), border-radius 999 px, shadow `0 8px 24px rgba(0,0,0,0.32)`. Not in-flow — `.project-page-frame` resets its `margin-top` accordingly.
- **Other shared components:** `CursorSpotlight` (cursor-tracked radial glow), `AskUserPanel` (interactive `ask_user`-tool controls above a composer), `GavelThinking` (AI loading indicator), `TokenUsagePill` (per-chat token total, gated on the `showTokenUsage` pref), `PageMasthead` (shared eyebrow/title/description header), `SwitchProjectLoader`, `ActivityMetrics`, `DangerZone`, `fileGlyph`.

### File previews

`FileThumbnail` resolves a poster URL (cached thumbnail or MIME glyph). `FilePreview` renders PDF (pdf.js) / image / video / text inline. Double-clicking a file in the Files page opens it in the **Doc Viewer** (`/doc-viewer`, `src/pages/DocViewer.jsx`) — a dedicated window with a `classify(mime, name)` dispatcher per mime type: photo/video get an "Extract text" OCR **lasso** tool (freeform Photoshop-style selection, clipped canvas → `lib/ocr.js`) with a persisted, resizable extraction-history panel (`lib/extractionHistory.js`); audio/video get a custom player whose **loudness-envelope canvas doubles as the seek control** (the full-file waveform is the scrubber — click / drag / arrow-key to seek; `role="slider"`), **YouTube-Music-style karaoke lyrics** in the player pane (the active timed segment highlights + auto-scrolls to centre), and a side AI-captions panel with **inline editing** of the generated transcript (fix mistranscriptions, recompute joined text). Captions come from `lib/transcribe.js` (Whisper) and are cached per file via `lib/captionsHistory.js`; the panel pushes edits/regenerations back to the player's lyrics live via an `onCaptionsChange` callback. `.docx` renders via `docx-preview` (lazy-imported, `lib/openDocxWindow.js`).

## Conventions

- **Styling:** plain CSS files alongside components (`Foo.jsx` + `Foo.css`); zero CSS-in-JS / Tailwind. Use only `var(--…)` from `tokens.css`. Adding a hard-coded hex is a smell — there's a semantic token for almost everything.
- **SVG icons:** inline JSX constants at the top of the file that uses them — no icon library. Stroke icons use `currentColor` so they inherit hover / active states.
- **`display: contents` wrappers** for synthetic event hosts that shouldn't add a layout box (e.g. Tooltip's `.tooltip-trigger-wrap`).
- **App base zoom is `1`** (`:root { zoom: 1 }` in `index.css`; `BASE_APP_ZOOM = 1` in `lib/appZoom.js`). The former 20% downscale (`zoom: 0.8`) was removed. The Settings "Display scale" preference still composes on top — webFrame zoom on Electron, inline CSS `zoom` on `<html>` on web (`platform.setAppZoom`). Any code that writes a **viewport** coordinate (`clientX`, a `getBoundingClientRect()` value) into a **CSS length** (a `style.left`/`transform`, an SVG coord, a `--spot-x`-style gradient var) must still pass it through `toLayoutPx()` — it's an identity at base zoom 1 but compensates the web display-scale (also CSS zoom). Ratios of two viewport values (percent splitters, seek bars) cancel the zoom and need no conversion.
- **Spotlight / cursor-tracked glows over portalled content** must use a **native** `addEventListener('mousemove')` on the host node, not React's `onMouseMove`. React synthetic events bubble along the React tree, so a handler on an ancestor whose visual children are `createPortal`'d elsewhere (e.g. the Doc Viewer advisor card, whose side panel portals into `.dv-advisor-slot`) never fires over that content — only on the host's own border/padding. Native listeners follow the real DOM tree where the portal nodes actually live.
- **FPS-independent cursor-chase easing.** Any rAF loop that eases a glow/spotlight toward the cursor must compute its lerp factor from the frame's timestamp delta, not a fixed per-frame constant, or the chase speed varies with refresh rate. Recipe: `factor = 1 - Math.pow(1 - EASE, dt / FRAME_60)` with `FRAME_60 = 1000/60` and `dt = clamp(ts - lastTs, …, 100)` (used by the Sidebar rail glow; `CursorSpotlight` instead snaps the box directly to the cursor each move).
- **5-stop animated gradient pill** (`.updates-banner-uptodate` and similar status pills): `linear-gradient(120deg, 5% / 16% / 18% / 16% / 5%)`, `background-size: 300% 100%`, 8 s shimmer keyframe; centred dot with `box-shadow: 0 0 0 6px ...` halo. Reusing this shape keeps "status pill" semantically consistent across the app.
- **Auth-derived display:** display name resolution is `user_metadata.full_name || user_metadata.name || user.email`. Avatar is `user_metadata.avatar_url` (Google) with a deterministic first-letter circle fallback (palette of 12 colours, djb2 hash on the id). Helper is duplicated across a few components (`Account.jsx`, `Sidebar.jsx`, ...) — keep them in sync if you change one.
- **Notification dedupe keys** are mandatory for anything that can fire repeatedly (uploads, downloads, errors). The notify() resolver coalesces / replaces / inserts based on category + dedupeKey, so a bad key spams the user.
- **Realtime subs** belong in a `useEffect` keyed on the resource id (projectId / userId), with a cleanup that unsubscribes. Don't open subs in render. Don't share a sub across providers — channel state is per-mount.
- **Fire-and-forget** is the default for non-critical writes (analytics, send-welcome, saveSidecar) — they return promises but call sites don't await. Errors are swallowed and the next operation retries naturally.
- **No tests, no lint.** Verify renderer changes via `npx esbuild --bundle --format=esm --platform=browser --loader:.jsx=jsx --loader:.js=jsx --loader:.css=empty --loader:.ico=empty --loader:.png=empty --loader:.jpg=empty --loader:.svg=empty --jsx=automatic --outfile=/tmp/check.js src/renderer.jsx` before claiming a change works.

## Configuration outside source

- `.env` — `VITE_SUPABASE_URL`, `VITE_SUPABASE_ANON_KEY` (Vite inlines at build time). Gitignored.
- **Supabase project `pntxlvhkqfryyyxlqytr`** (eu-west-1, organization `docvex.ro`). Modify schema via `claude_ai_Supabase` MCP tools.
- **Supabase dashboard, not in code:** Google OAuth provider config (client id / secret), `docvex://auth/callback` and the web origin registered as redirect URLs, the SMTP for email Edge Functions.
- **Edge Function secrets (Supabase dashboard → Edge Functions → Secrets), not in code:** `RESEND_API_KEY` (email functions); `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY` (`legal-ai` + `doc-ai` OCR — without it the Newsletter AI line falls back to a computed line, ingest 500s, and OCR fails); `OPENAI_API_KEY` (`doc-ai` Whisper transcription — set since 2026-06-18); `LEGAL_INGEST_SECRET` (optional — guards `legal-ai`'s `ingest` action; while unset, ingest returns 403); `LEGAL_AI_MODEL` (optional — overrides the default `claude-opus-4-7`, e.g. `claude-haiku-4-5` to cut digest cost).
- **Google Cloud Console:** OAuth consent screen must be User Type **External** (Internal blocks `@gmail.com` testers with `org_internal` 403). Authorized redirect URI = `https://pntxlvhkqfryyyxlqytr.supabase.co/auth/v1/callback`.
- **macOS signing env (release-time, not in code):** `APPLE_SIGNING_IDENTITY`
  (a `Developer ID Application: … (TEAMID)` cert in the login keychain) enables
  the Developer ID + notarization path in `forge.config.js`; pair it with notary
  creds — `APPLE_API_KEY`/`APPLE_API_KEY_ID`/`APPLE_API_ISSUER` **or**
  `APPLE_ID`/`APPLE_APP_SPECIFIC_PASSWORD`/`APPLE_TEAM_ID`. Unset ⇒ ad-hoc
  fallback (Gatekeeper-blocked). See the code-signing note above.
- **GitHub:** `GITHUB_TOKEN` (PAT, `public_repo` scope) required for `npm run publish` — set via `[Environment]::SetEnvironmentVariable("GITHUB_TOKEN", ..., "User")` or per-session `$env:GITHUB_TOKEN = ...`. VSCode integrated terminals cache env vars from launch; restart the whole VSCode window after setting persistently.

## Scripts (`scripts/`)

| Script | Purpose |
| --- | --- |
| `sync-readme-version.mjs` | Bumps the version string in README.md to match package.json. Runs in the `version` lifecycle. |
| `build-courts.mjs` | Rebuilds `src/lib/courts.json` (every court portal.just.ro's service knows, with a readable label) from the service's own WSDL. The place in each label is written as it writes itself — the id's "ALBAIULIA" / "SANNICOLAULMARE" looked up in the app's gazetteer (`lib/roLocalities.json`, counties from `lib/identities`; three spellings the gazetteer writes differently are mapped by hand, and a sector is "Sectorul N București"); a place it cannot name is capitalised as spelt and reported. `--relabel` rewrites the labels of the ids already in the file, no network. Run when a court is added or renamed, then release. |
| `build-caen-old.mjs` | Rebuilds `src/lib/caenRev2.json` + `caenRev1.json` — the OLD revisions, each the same tree shape as Rev. 3's — from `CAEN-Rev.2_structura-completa.pdf` + `Corespondenta-CAEN-Rev.1-CAEN-Rev.2.pdf` (insse.ro/cms/ro/caen; download with `curl -k`, the site's certificate chain is incomplete) and the text of Ordinul INS nr. 601/2002 (the only official Rev. 1 nomenclature — fetched from legislatie.just.ro's free service, or passed as a saved .txt). Reads the PDFs with the project's pdf.js, merges a code and its name printed a point apart, drops page numbers, drops the five Rev. 2 codes the INS table names that Rev. 2 never had (its errata, listed in the script), drops Rev. 1's subsections. Verifies the counts (Rev. 2: 21 / 88 / 272 / 615; Rev. 1: 17 / 62 / 224 / 514). |
| `build-caen.mjs` | Rebuilds `src/lib/caenRev3.json` + `caenRev3Notes.json` from the INS files (insse.ro/cms/ro/caen: `Note-explicative-CAEN-Rev.3-*.xlsx` and `Tabel-Corespondenta-CAEN-Rev.2-CAEN-Rev.3_*.xlsx`; download them first — the site's certificate chain is incomplete, so Node's fetch refuses it). Blank Rev. 3 cells in the correspondence table are MERGED cells and inherit the code above. Run when INS publishes a new edition, then release. |
| `post-release.mjs` | Runs after `npm version`: `git push --follow-tags`, `electron-forge publish`, `generate-release-notes.mjs`. |
| `generate-release-notes.mjs` | Summarises commits since the previous tag via the `claude` CLI and PATCHes the draft GitHub release body. Best-effort, never fails the release. |
| `make-mac-zips.mjs` | Zips the packaged `.app` bundles, preserving framework symlinks (archiver, not cross-zip). **On macOS** it first copies to a non-iCloud temp dir, `xattr -cr`, `codesign --force --deep --sign -`, and `--verify --strict` (fails the build if invalid). `MAC_ZIP_VERSION` overrides the filename version. Non-Mac hosts can't sign → those zips crash on Apple Silicon. |
| `publish-mac-zips.mjs` | Release-time companion: packages both darwin arches, runs make-mac-zips, uploads the two zips to the draft release (deletes same-named assets first for idempotency). Called from `post-release.mjs`. |
| `fix-mac-release.mjs` (`npm run fix:mac`) | One-shot repair for an existing release's macOS assets — **must run on a Mac.** Resolves the target tag (default: latest), rebuilds both arches stamped with the release version (`DOCVEX_APP_VERSION`), re-signs + verifies + zips (`MAC_ZIP_VERSION`), and replaces the release's darwin zips. Needs `GITHUB_TOKEN`. |

## Release notes style (user preference)

Four sections (`Added` / `Changed` / `Removed` / `Misc`), no emojis, plain English understandable to non-programmers. This overrides the auto-generator's default format — adjust the prompt in `generate-release-notes.mjs` if the template drifts.

Reference docs live at `C:\Users\Luca\Desktop\docvex\`:

- `Docvex_AI.pdf` — product vision and feature spec
- `text1.txt`, `text2.txt` — business strategy and target market notes (Romanian)

Read these when reasoning about features, product decisions, positioning, or anything related to "why" we're building something. Code lives in this repo; "why" lives in that folder.
